\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{E}=0,\;\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{B}=0,\;\mathbf{E}\perp\mathbf{B}.

Conclusion:

Even
without Maxwell equations
,
the universal reflection/refraction phenomena
(
A_{2}
,
A_{3}
) + the
empirical
fact that
no
separate longitudinal mode is observed
can be recast to show:

Light in non-conducting, source-free media is transverse.

Appendix F
From Wave Optics to Quantum Optics

F.1
Wave Optics

These axioms are the same as defined in Section
E.2
.

F.2
Quantum Optics

Quantum Optics provides a full quantum-mechanical description of light (and its interaction with matter). It explains phenomena beyond the scope of purely classical or semiclassical models, such as single-photon interference, vacuum fluctuations, photon antibunching, and entanglement. In a linear, source-free medium (no free charges or currents, negligible nonlinearities), one can formulate a minimal set of axioms that capture the essence of quantum electromagnetic fields. These axioms are both sufficient (completeness) and mutually independent for describing the core phenomena of quantum optics.

F.2.1
Axioms

1.

Field Quantization.

\hat{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbf{r},t),\;\hat{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{r},t)~\longrightarrow~\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}~\text{with}~[\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}]=\delta_{kk^{\prime}},

The electromagnetic field is promoted to an operator in a Hilbert space.
Each mode
k
is described by annihilation
\hat{a}_{k}
and creation
\hat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}
operators satisfying canonical commutation relations.
This quantization underpins discrete-photon (Fock) states, coherent states, squeezed states, and more.

2.

Unitary Evolution (Schrödinger or Heisenberg Picture).

i\hbar\,\frac{d}{dt}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>\;=\;\hat{H}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>,

In the Schrödinger picture, the quantum state
\left|\Psi(t)\right>
of the field evolves according to the Hamiltonian
\hat{H}
.
In the Heisenberg picture, field operators evolve instead, while states remain fixed.
Either formulation captures how quantum states of light propagate in space or time and how they interact with matter.

3.

Quantum Measurement Postulate.

\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{outcomes}=\text{eigenvalues of }\hat{\mathcal{O}},\hskip 9.24994ptp(\text{outcome}=\lambda)=\|\hat{P}_{\lambda}\left|\Psi\right>\|^{2},

Physical observables (e.g., photon number, field quadratures) correspond to Hermitian operators.
Measurement outcomes are given by their eigenvalues, with probabilities determined by the Born rule.
Post-measurement states follow the standard projection or, more generally, a completely positive (CPTP) map.
This postulate underlies single-photon detection, quantum state collapse (or state update),
and all interference/entanglement measurements in quantum optics.

F.2.2
Completeness

These three axioms form a complete basis for describing all key effects in quantum optics:

1.

Field Quantization
allows us to represent light in terms of discrete or continuous quantum states (Fock, coherent, squeezed, etc.), capturing inherently quantum behaviors like vacuum fluctuations and photon antibunching.

2.

Unitary Evolution
governs the time evolution and interactions of these quantum states, enabling analyses of multi-photon interference, beam-splitter transformations, and quantum entanglement generation.

3.

Quantum Measurement
accounts for how photodetection, homodyne detection, or other measurement schemes yield observable outcomes and how the act of measurement influences or “collapses” the quantum state.

Together, they suffice to derive virtually all standard quantum-optical phenomena, from single-photon interference to multipartite entanglement protocols.

F.2.3
Independence

1.

Field Quantization
is not inferable from the unitary evolution or measurement principles alone: one may have quantum evolution and measurement in other (non-photonic) systems without imposing photon creation/annihilation operators or the discrete energy levels of the electromagnetic field modes.

2.

Unitary Evolution
cannot be deduced from quantization and measurement alone, as it prescribes
how
states evolve in time under a Hamiltonian and is not captured by just asserting that a quantum field exists with certain commutation relations.

3.

Quantum Measurement Postulate
does not automatically follow from field quantization and unitary dynamics; it explicitly states
how
measurement outcomes arise and how they probabilistically update the state of the system.

Hence, none of the three axioms can be derived from the others, ensuring their logical independence in describing the quantum nature of light.

F.3
Transformations

F.3.1
Axiom 1

Goal:

Transform the classical
Wave Equation
(Axiom 1) into the
Field Quantization
postulate

Q_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbf{r},t),\;\hat{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{r},t)~\longrightarrow~\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}~\text{with}~[\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}]=\delta_{kk^{\prime}}.

Initial Set (Wave Equation).

S_{0}=\{W_{1}\},\hskip 9.24994ptW_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\nabla^{2}\mathbf{E}-\frac{n^{2}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{E}}{\partial t^{2}}=0,\;\;\nabla^{2}\mathbf{B}-\frac{n^{2}}{c^{2}}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{B}}{\partial t^{2}}=0.

Rewrite Step 1: Mode Expansion.

Decompose each classical field into a sum (or integral) of normal modes:

\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{r},t)=\sum_{k}\Bigl[E_{k}(t)\,\boldsymbol{\phi}_{k}(\mathbf{r})\Bigr],\hskip 9.24994pt\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{r},t)=\sum_{k}\Bigl[B_{k}(t)\,\boldsymbol{\psi}_{k}(\mathbf{r})\Bigr].

Symbolically,

W_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{1}^{\prime}:\;\{\text{Mode Decomposition}\}

S_{1}=\{\,W_{1}^{\prime}\}.

Rewrite Step 2: Promote Amplitudes to Operators.

Replace each classical amplitude
E_{k}(t)
,
B_{k}(t)
by quantum
operators

\hat{E}_{k}(t),\hat{B}_{k}(t)
.

W_{1}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{1}^{\prime\prime}:\;\bigl\{\hat{E}_{k}(t),\;\hat{B}_{k}(t)\bigr\}.

S_{2}=\{\,W_{1}^{\prime\prime}\}.

Rewrite Step 3: Canonical Commutation.

Identify
\hat{E}_{k},\hat{B}_{k}
as linear combinations of
\hat{a}_{k}
and
\hat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}
, the annihilation/creation operators satisfying

\bigl[\hat{a}_{k},\,\hat{a}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\bigr]=\delta_{kk^{\prime}}.

This step imposes the canonical
quantization condition
on the field modes.

W_{1}^{\prime\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;Q_{1}:\;\bigl\{\hat{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbf{r},t),\;\hat{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{r},t)~\longrightarrow~\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}~\text{with}~[\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}]=\delta_{kk^{\prime}}\bigr\}.

S_{3}=\{\,Q_{1}\}.

Final Set (Quantum Optics Axiom 1: Field Quantization).

\boxed{Q_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbf{r},t),\;\hat{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{r},t)~\longrightarrow~\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}~\text{with}~[\hat{a}_{k},\hat{a}_{k^{\prime}}^{\dagger}]=\delta_{kk^{\prime}}.}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact):

\underbrace{\{W_{1}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{W_{1}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{W_{1}^{\prime\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{Q_{1}\}}_{S_{3}}.

F.3.2
Axiom 2

Goal:
Show how
Boundary Conditions
(Axiom 2 of Wave Optics) can be reinterpreted to yield the
Unitary Evolution
postulate:

Q_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pti\hbar\,\frac{d}{dt}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>=\hat{H}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>,\hskip 9.24994pt\text{(or Heisenberg picture for operators).}

Initial Set (Boundary Conditions).

S_{0}=\{W_{2}\},\hskip 9.24994ptW_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{cases}\epsilon_{1}\,\mathbf{E}_{\perp}\big|_{1}-\epsilon_{2}\,\mathbf{E}_{\perp}\big|_{2}=\sigma,\\
\mathbf{B}_{\perp}\;\text{cont.},\\
\mathbf{E}_{\parallel},\mathbf{B}_{\parallel}\;\text{cont.}\end{cases}

Rewrite Step 1: From Interface Continuity to Global Consistency.

In classical wave language, matching boundary conditions at
every interface
ensures a single, consistent solution
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{B}
across the entire optical system. Symbolically,

W_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{2}^{\prime}:\;\text{(Global single solution across all regions)}.

S_{1}=\{W_{2}^{\prime}\}.

Rewrite Step 2: Single Global Solution
\to
Single Global State.

In quantum language, a
single global solution
of Maxwell’s equations becomes a
single global quantum state

\left|\Psi\right>
. Rather than specifying field values in each region, we specify the
amplitudes
(or wavefunction) for the entire configuration. Symbolically,

W_{2}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{2}^{\prime\prime}:\;\left|\Psi\right>\;\text{is a global state describing the field.}

S_{2}=\{W_{2}^{\prime\prime}\}.

Rewrite Step 3: Impose Hamiltonian Dynamics.

To encode
how
the field evolves in time (including how boundary interactions might reflect/refract quantum amplitudes), we introduce the Hamiltonian
\hat{H}
. The time evolution of
\left|\Psi(t)\right>
is then:

i\hbar\,\frac{d}{dt}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>=\hat{H}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>.

Thus,

W_{2}^{\prime\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;Q_{2}:\;(\text{Unitary Evolution}).

S_{3}=\{Q_{2}\}.

Final Set (Quantum Optics Axiom 2: Unitary Evolution).

\boxed{Q_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pti\hbar\,\frac{d}{dt}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>=\hat{H}\,\left|\Psi(t)\right>.}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact):

\underbrace{\{W_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{W_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{W_{2}^{\prime\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{Q_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}.

F.3.3
Axiom 3

Goal:
Starting with the
Transverse Nature of Light
(Axiom 3), derive the
Quantum Measurement Postulate
:

Q_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\;\;\text{outcomes}=\text{eigenvalues of }\hat{\mathcal{O}},\;\;p(\text{outcome})=\|\hat{P}_{\text{outcome}}\left|\Psi\right>\|^{2},

which is the Born rule for measurement in quantum optics.

Initial Set (Transverse Nature).

S_{0}=\{\,W_{3}\},\hskip 9.24994ptW_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{E}=0,\;\;\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{B}=0,\;\;\mathbf{E}\perp\mathbf{B}.

Rewrite Step 1: Identify Independent Components.

Since
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
are purely transverse, each has only two independent polarization components.
Moreover,
E
and
B
are directly related to each other. Symbolically,

W_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{3}^{\prime}:\;\text{(Only 2 degrees of freedom per mode.)}

S_{1}=\{W_{3}^{\prime}\}.

Rewrite Step 2: Associate Observables (Polarization, Quadratures).

In a quantum field theory context, each transverse degree of freedom becomes an
observable
(e.g. a polarization basis or field quadrature). Symbolically,

W_{3}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{3}^{\prime\prime}:\;\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha},\;\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\beta},

where
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}
and
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}
might be (for example) two orthogonal polarization or quadrature operators.

S_{2}=\{W_{3}^{\prime\prime}\}.

Rewrite Step 3: Impose Measurement Postulates.

To fully specify how outcomes of these observables
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha},\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}
appear in experiments, we introduce the
Quantum Measurement Postulate
, i.e. that these operators are Hermitian, their eigenvalues are the possible measurement results, and outcome probabilities follow the Born rule. Symbolically,

W_{3}^{\prime\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;Q_{3}:\;(\text{Hermitian operators, eigenvalue outcomes, Born rule}).

S_{3}=\{Q_{3}\}.

Final Set (Quantum Optics Axiom 3: Measurement).

\boxed{Q_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\;\;\text{outcomes = eigenvalues of }\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\;\;p(\text{outcome}=\lambda)=\|\hat{P}_{\lambda}\left|\Psi\right>\|^{2}}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact):

\underbrace{\{W_{3}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{W_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{W_{3}^{\prime\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{Q_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}.

F.3.4
Summary of the Transformations

1.

Wave Equation

\to

Field Quantization
:

W_{1}\;\Longrightarrow\;Q_{1}.

By recasting the classical fields as operator-valued mode expansions, we impose canonical commutation and obtain creation/annihilation operators.

2.

Boundary Conditions

\to

Unitary Evolution
:

W_{2}\;\Longrightarrow\;Q_{2}.

The requirement of consistent field solutions across interfaces leads to a single global quantum state and the Hamiltonian-based time evolution.

3.

Transverse Nature

\to

Quantum Measurement
:

W_{3}\;\Longrightarrow\;Q_{3}.

The restriction to transverse degrees of freedom becomes the quantized operator framework, requiring a measurement postulate (Hermitian observables and Born rule) to predict experimental outcomes.

Hence, each of the three
Wave Optics Axioms
can be “lifted” to its
Quantum Optics
counterpart.

Appendix G
From Analog Electronics to Digital Computing

G.1
Analog Electronics

Analog electronics deals with the behavior of circuits comprising continuous signals, resistors, capacitors, inductors, and active or passive elements, all obeying laws in the time or frequency domain. Below is a minimal and complete set of axioms for
linear
analog electronics, each of which is independent of the others. Together, they suffice to derive the standard behavior of linear circuits: Kirchhoff’s rules, device equations, superposition, frequency response, and more.

G.1.1
Axioms

1.

Kirchhoff’s Laws.

\text{(KCL)}\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{n}I_{n}(t)\;=\;0,\hskip 18.49988pt\text{(KVL)}\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{n}V_{n}(t)\;=\;0.

Statement.

At any node, the algebraic sum of currents is zero (Kirchhoff’s Current Law, KCL); around any loop, the algebraic sum of voltages is zero (Kirchhoff’s Voltage Law, KVL).

2.

Linear Constitutive Relations.

\displaystyle\text{Resistor:}

\displaystyle V(t)\;=\;R\,I(t),

\displaystyle\text{Capacitor:}

\displaystyle I(t)\;=\;C\,\frac{dV}{dt},

\displaystyle\text{Inductor:}

\displaystyle V(t)\;=\;L\,\frac{dI}{dt},\hskip 9.24994pt\ldots

Statement.

Each (passive) two-terminal component relates its voltage and current via a linear, time-invariant (LTI) differential equation. In the simplest cases: resistors (Ohm’s law), capacitors, and inductors. More generally, any linear element can be described by an impedance or admittance
Z(\omega)
or
Y(\omega)
.

G.1.2
Completeness

These axioms suffice to describe the fundamental behavior of all
linear
analog circuits:

1.

Kirchhoff’s Laws.
Provide global constraints on the voltages and currents, ensuring that charges and energy flows are conserved throughout the circuit.

2.

Linear Constitutive Relations.
Define how each circuit element (resistor, capacitor, inductor, etc.) responds to voltage and current, laying the foundation for time-domain and frequency-domain analyses.

G.1.3
Independence

1.

Kirchhoff’s Laws
cannot be derived from the constitutive relations or superposition alone; they express fundamental conservation principles that apply regardless of how individual circuit elements behave.

2.

Linear Constitutive Relations
are not deducible from Kirchhoff’s Laws or superposition. Kirchhoff’s Laws and superposition only constrain how voltages/currents combine or sum at nodes and loops; they do not dictate the specific linear equations linking voltage and current of a single component.

Hence, none of the two axioms can be derived from the other, ensuring their mutual independence in describing linear analog electronics.

G.2
Digital Electronics

Digital electronics models signals, circuits, and systems in terms of discrete (often binary) states, representing logical operations and finite-state behavior. Below is a minimal set of axioms for
classical digital electronics
. Each axiom is independent of the others, and collectively, they suffice to derive standard results in digital logic, including combinational and sequential circuit analysis.

G.2.1
Axioms

1.

Binary Signal States.

\text{Each signal }X(t)\text{ can take on only two distinct values, }\{\,0,1\},

representing logical ‘false’ or ‘true.’

Statement.

All digital signals are confined to two nominal voltage levels (or two symbols). Intermediate values or analog considerations are assumed to be beyond the logical model.

2.

Boolean Gate Operations.

\forall\,(\text{AND, OR, NOT, etc.}):\hskip 9.24994pt\text{output}=f_{\text{gate}}(\text{inputs}),

Statement.

Each combinational logic element (gate) transforms its input bit(s) according to a Boolean function (truth table). For instance, an AND gate outputs ‘1’ if and only if
all
inputs are ‘1’; a NOT gate flips ‘0’ to ‘1’ and vice versa.

3.

Synchronous State Update (Clocked Memory).

\text{Flip-flops, registers: }Q(t+\Delta t)=F\bigl(Q(t),\,X(t)\bigr)\hskip 9.24994pt\text{on clock edge.}

Statement.

Sequential circuits store and update states at discrete time intervals (clock cycles). Flip-flops or registers sample input signals and produce outputs synchronized to a clock, thus forming the basis of finite-state machines.

G.2.2
Completeness

These three axioms suffice to describe the key aspects of classical digital circuits:

1.

Binary Signal States.

Establishes the discrete, two-level (0/1) nature of signals, enabling logical (rather than continuous) interpretation of circuit behavior.

2.

Boolean Gate Operations.

Captures how combinational logic processes input bits via gates (AND, OR, NOT, NAND, NOR, etc.). Permits constructing arbitrary Boolean functions.

3.

Synchronous State Update.

Introduces memory elements and timing, forming sequential circuits. This encompasses registers, counters, finite-state machines, and synchronous system design.

G.2.3
Independence

1.

Binary Signal States
is not derivable from Boolean gates or clocked memory alone. Gates and flip-flops could, in principle, operate on multi-valued logic; the two-level nature of digital signals must be stated independently.

2.

Boolean Gate Operations
cannot be inferred from the binary state axiom or from the presence of a clock or memory. Even with two-level signals and timing, one must specifically define
how
the inputs map to outputs for each gate.

3.

Synchronous State Update
does not follow from binary signals or Boolean operations. One can have purely combinational (stateless) logic or asynchronous designs unless the notion of clocked memory is explicitly introduced.

Hence, none of these three axioms can be derived from the others, ensuring their mutual independence in describing classical digital electronics.

G.3
Transformation

G.3.1
Axiom 1

Goal:

Transform a subset of analog axioms

\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Kirchhoff's Laws},\\
A_{2}&:\text{Linear Constitutive Relations.},\end{aligned}

into the
Digital Electronics
statement:

D_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Each signal can take on only two distinct (logical) values, }\{0,1\}.

All intermediate steps are small symbolic rewrites that gradually evolve the original set into the final one.

Initial Set of Axioms (Analog, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{n}I_{n}(t)=0,\;\;\sum_{n}V_{n}(t)=0\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Kirchhoff's Current/Voltage Laws}),

\hskip 9.24994ptA_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Resistor: }V=R\,I,\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Capacitor: }I=C\,dV/dt,\dots\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{linear devices});

Here,
A_{1}
enforces basic conservation of charge/energy in circuits, while
A_{2}
implies that circuit responses to multiple inputs sum linearly (linearity principle).

Rewrite Step 1: Introduce Superposition as Separation of Inputs.

A_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Circuit signals can be decomposed into partial contributions (modes).}

Because Kirchoff’s laws (
A_{1}
) and the voltage-current relationships for circuit elements (
A_{2}
) are linear, the overall circuit exhibits linear behavior (superposition principle). Hence, the response of a system to an input can be decomposed as a response to several other inputs. Symbolically,

\forall\,(\text{inputs})\hskip 9.24994ptV_{\text{total}}(t)=\sum_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}(t),

and the circuit processes each
V_{\alpha}(t)
independently (linearly).
Hence,

S_{0}=\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}.

No mention yet of restricting signals to two levels; we have merely restated superposition in a “decomposed signals” language.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,A_{1},\;A_{2}^{\prime}\}}

where

A_{1}:\;\text{Kirchhoff's Laws (KCL, KVL)},

A_{2}^{\prime}:\;\text{Signals decompose into linear sums of simpler components.}

Rewrite Step 2: Enforce Threshold-like Nonlinearity at the Output Node.

We now add a (conceptual) “hard” threshold in the circuit’s output stage, imposing that any resulting output voltage
\widetilde{V}(t)
is
forced
to saturate to one of two levels:

\widetilde{V}(t)=\begin{cases}V_{\mathrm{LOW}},&\text{if underlying analog }V_{\text{net}}(t)<V_{\mathrm{th}},\\
V_{\mathrm{HIGH}},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Symbolically,

A_{1},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}^{\prime}:

where
A_{1}^{\prime}
is Kirchhoff’s Laws
plus
a saturating active device, ensuring only two output levels can appear.

S_{1}=\{\,A_{1},\;A_{2}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}.

A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Kirchhoff's Laws})+(\text{bistable saturation device}).

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}}

with

A_{1}^{\prime}:\;\text{KCL,KVL plus saturating output stage},

A_{2}^{\prime}:\;\text{Signals still superimpose in the linear sub-block}.

Rewrite Step 3: Abstract Away All Intermediate Voltages.

Once we accept that any real output node can only
end up
at
\{V_{\mathrm{LOW}},V_{\mathrm{HIGH}}\}
, we effectively identify these two saturations with
\{0,1\}
. Symbolically,

(A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}^{\prime})\;\longrightarrow\;D_{1}:\;\{\text{Binary states only}\}.

Hence:

S_{2}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,D_{1}\},

where

D_{1}:\;\text{All signals are 0 or 1 (two distinct logical levels).}

Final Set (Digital Axiom 1, Binary Signal States):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{D_{1}:\;\{0,1\}\text{ logic levels}\Bigr\}}

I.e. each output node saturates to LOW or HIGH, representing a discrete binary value.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,D_{1}\}}_{S_{3}}.

G.3.2
Axiom 2

Goal:

Starting from the two
key analog axioms

A_{1}:\;\text{Kirchhoff's Laws},\hskip 9.24994ptA_{2}:\;\text{Linear Constitutive Relations},

we wish to derive (in small symbolic steps) the
Digital Axiom 2
:

D_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Combinational logic gates (AND, OR, NOT, etc.)
perform}

Boolean operations on inputs.

Initial Set of Axioms (Analog Subset):

S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\;A_{2}\bigr\}

where

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{n}I_{n}(t)=0,\;\;\sum_{n}V_{n}(t)=0

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Resistor: }V=R\,I,\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Capacitor: }I=C\,dV/dt,\dots\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{linear devices});

Rewrite Step 1: Introduce Superposition.

S_{0}=\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}.

This rewrite step is identical to the one leading to axiom 1. Hence, for brevity, it was not repeated here.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,A_{1},\;A_{2}^{\prime}\}}

where

A_{1}:\;\text{Kirchhoff's Laws (KCL, KVL)},

A_{2}^{\prime}:\;\text{Signals decompose into linear sums of simpler components.}

Rewrite Step 2: Combine Linear Elements into a Single Transfer Function.

A_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;B_{2}:\hskip 9.24994ptH(\omega)=\frac{V_{\text{out}}(\omega)}{V_{\text{in}}(\omega)},

the net transfer function. By cascade/feedback of linear devices (resistors, capacitors, inductors), we still have a linear system, so

V_{\text{out}}(\omega)=H(\omega)\,V_{\text{in}}(\omega).

Symbolically,

S_{1}=\{A_{2},A_{2}^{\prime}\}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{2}=\{\,B_{2},A_{2}^{\prime}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,B_{2},\;A_{2}^{\prime}\}}

where

B_{2}:\hskip 9.24994ptV_{\text{out}}(\omega)=H(\omega)\,V_{\text{in}}(\omega),\hskip 9.24994ptA_{2}^{\prime}:\;\text{Superposition Principle.}

Rewrite Step 3: Impose Binary Inputs/Outputs on the Transfer Function.

With the
binary signal
idea from
D_{1}
, assume each input line is either 0 or 1, and the output must also saturate to 0 or 1. The linear transfer function now effectively
collapses
into a truth table:

(B_{2},A_{2}^{\prime})\;\longrightarrow\;B_{3}:\hskip 9.24994ptf_{\text{gate}}:\;(0,0)\mapsto X,\dots

For instance, a saturating amplifier with certain resistor networks might implement

\text{AND},\text{OR},\text{NOT}
, etc. once we interpret
V_{\mathrm{LOW}}\to 0
,
V_{\mathrm{HIGH}}\to 1
.

Hence,

S_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{3}=\{\,B_{3}\},

where

B_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Each linear sub-block + saturations }\implies\text{Boolean function table}.

Rewrite Step 4: Conclude that the Circuit is a Boolean Gate.

Once we identify the input signals with
\{0,1\}
and interpret each sub-block’s output in the same
\{0,1\}
-based manner, the entire circuit
\to

gate
operation:

D_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Gate}):\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Output}=f(\text{Inputs}),

where
f
is a Boolean function (AND, OR, NOT, etc.).

S_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{4}=\{\,D_{2}\}.

Thus we obtain:

D_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Boolean gate operations on binary signals}.

Final Set (Digital Axiom 2, Gate Operations):

\boxed{S_{4}=\Bigl\{D_{2}:\;\text{Each combinational block is a Boolean function on }\{0,1\}^{n}\Bigr\}}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact View):

\underbrace{\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\,A_{2},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\,B_{2},\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\,B_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\,D_{2}\}}_{S_{4}}.

G.3.3
Axiom 3

Goal:

Show a step-by-step transformation that leads from

A_{1}:\,\text{Kirchhoff's Laws},\hskip 9.24994ptA_{2}:\,\text{Linear Constitutive Relations}

to the
Digital Axiom 3
:

D_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Flip-flops, registers, and memory elements
update synchronously}

\text{on a clock,
}Q(t+\Delta t)=F\bigl(Q(t),X(t)\bigr).

Initial Setup (Analog + Some Active Components):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\;A_{2},\;\text{Active Amplification}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{n}I_{n}(t)=0,\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{n}V_{n}(t)=0,

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994ptV=R\,I,\;I=C\,dV/dt,\dots

Active Amplification:
we allow operational amplifiers or transistors to provide gain/feedback, which can form bistable or multi-stable circuits.

Rewrite Step 1: Bistable Circuit (Latch).

Using op-amps or transistor pairs (e.g. an SR latch), we combine the linear elements (A2) with Kirchhoff’s Laws (A1) to form a
bistable
circuit. Symbolically,

(A_{1},\;A_{2},\;\text{Active Amplification})\;\longrightarrow\;B_{1}:\;\text{Circuit has two stable states.}

Hence,

S_{0}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{1}=\{\,B_{1}\}.

B_{1}:\hskip 9.24994ptQ\in\{\mathrm{StateA},\,\mathrm{StateB}\},\;\text{no intermediate stable states exist}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\Bigl\{B_{1}:\;\text{two stable states in a closed feedback loop}\Bigr\}}

Rewrite Step 2: Add Clock Signal for Controlled Transition.

A pure bistable latch can switch states at any time if inputs cross certain thresholds. To enforce
synchronous
updates, we introduce a global
clock
line that enables the latch to sample inputs only at discrete moments (the clock edge). Symbolically,

B_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;B_{2}:\;\text{(Clocked Flip-Flop)}.

S_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{2}=\{\;B_{2}\}.

B_{2}:\hskip 9.24994ptQ(t+\Delta t)=F\bigl(Q(t),X(t)\bigr)\;\text{only at clock rising/falling edge}.

Rewrite Step 3: Recognize Finite-State Memory Element.

Once the latch is governed by a clock, it becomes a
sequential
device (e.g. D flip-flop, JK flip-flop, register bit). We interpret
\{Q(t),Q(t+\Delta t)\}
as a finite-state machine:

D_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Synchronous state update: }Q(t+\Delta t)=F\bigl(Q(t),X(t)\bigr).

Hence,

S_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{3}=\{\,D_{3}\}.

We have thus arrived at the digital notion of clocked memory or flip-flops.

Final Set (Digital Axiom 3, Synchronous Update):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{D_{3}:\;Q(t+\Delta t)=F\bigl(Q(t),X(t)\bigr)\Bigr\}.}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact View):

\underbrace{\{\,A_{1},A_{2},\;\text{Active Amplification}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\,B_{1}:\text{Bistable Latch}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;

\longrightarrow\underbrace{\{\,B_{2}:\text{Clocked Flip-Flop}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\,D_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}.

G.3.4
Summary of Transformations (Analog to Digital)

1.

From Kirchhoff + Linear Relations to Binary Signal States
:

\{A_{1},A_{2}\}\;\Longrightarrow\;D_{1}.

By adding a threshold-based saturation stage, continuous voltages are forced into LOW or HIGH, yielding discrete
\{0,1\}
.

2.

From Linear Devices to Boolean Gate Operations
:

\{A_{2},A_{3}\}\;\Longrightarrow\;D_{2}.

Once inputs/outputs are binary, linear sub-blocks with saturations become truth tables, i.e. Boolean gates (AND, OR, NOT, etc.).

3.

From Bistable Circuits to Synchronous State Updates
:

\{A_{1},A_{2}\}\;\Longrightarrow\;D_{3}.

Incorporating active components and clock signals transforms a latch into a flip-flop or register, enforcing synchronous updates of the internal state.

Hence, each of the three
Analog Electronics Axioms
can be “lifted” or transformed into a corresponding
Digital Electronics Axiom
by introducing threshold logic, binary saturation, and clock-based sequencing. This parallels a stepwise progression where continuous analog phenomena become discrete binary states, Boolean logic, and synchronous finite-state machines.

Appendix H
From Digital Computing to Quantum Computing

H.1
Digital Computing

Digital computing, at its core, can be captured by a minimal set of foundational principles. These axioms formalize how
discrete
information is manipulated via
symbolic
rules and
finite
internal states, ensuring universal computation capability. Below is one way (more theoretical and less hardware-based than in the section above) to present a set of three axioms that are sufficient to describe classical digital computation and are mutually independent.

H.1.1
Axioms

1.

Discrete Symbol Representation.

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{All information is encoded using a finite alphabet }\Sigma\text{ (e.g.\ bits).}

Statement.

At the lowest level, any digital system uses a finite set of distinct symbols
\Sigma
(e.g.
\{0,1\}
in binary) to represent data and instructions. Intermediate analog effects are
not
part of the logical model; signals are interpreted only as symbols from
\Sigma
.

2.

Finite Control and State Transitions.

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994ptS\xrightarrow{\delta}S^{\prime},\hskip 9.24994ptS,\,S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S},\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{S}\text{ finite},\hskip 9.24994pt\delta:\mathcal{S}\times\Sigma\to\mathcal{S}.

Statement.

A digital computer at any instant is in one of a finite number of internal states
\mathcal{S}
. Input symbols (from
\Sigma
), along with the current state
S
, determine the next state
S^{\prime}
via a well-defined transition function
\delta
. This principle underlies finite-state machines, CPU instruction cycles, and other sequential structures.

3.

Algorithmic Universality (Effective Computability).

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,\mathcal{U}:\;\forall\,f\in\mathcal{C},\;\exists\,p\in\Sigma^{*},\;\forall\,x\in\Sigma^{*}:\;\mathcal{U}(p,x)=f(x).

Statement.

There exists a
universal
mechanism
\mathcal{U}
(e.g. a programmable processor) such that for every
effectively definable
function
f
over the alphabet
\Sigma
, there is a finite “program”
p\in\Sigma^{*}
making
\mathcal{U}
emulate
f
. In practice, a minimal instruction set or abstract universal device suffices to show that all computable functions can be implemented.

Interpretation of Symbolic Form:

-
\Sigma^{*}
is the set of all finite strings over
\Sigma
.

-
\mathcal{C}
is the set of all
effectively definable
(computable) functions
\Sigma^{*}\to\Sigma^{*}
.

-
\mathcal{U}(p,x)
denotes the output produced by the universal mechanism
\mathcal{U}
when given “program”
p
and input
x
.

Thus
A_{3}
asserts that
\mathcal{U}
can reproduce
any
desired computable function, once given the appropriate program.

H.1.2
Completeness

These three axioms account for the essential ingredients of classical digital computing:

1.

Discrete Symbol Representation

guarantees that all data and instructions are handled as symbolic tokens from a finite set (bits, characters, etc.), enabling unambiguous, reproducible manipulation.

2.

Finite Control and State Transitions

encodes how a digital system moves from one configuration to another in discrete time steps (or events). This underpins everything from simple finite-state controllers to CPU instruction cycles.

3.

Algorithmic Universality

confirms that by combining discrete symbols and a finite control mechanism, one can implement
every
computable function (assuming sufficient memory/time resources). This is the essence of universal computability.

Together, they suffice to describe how classical digital machines store information (via discrete symbols), update their state (via finite transitions), and perform any computable algorithm.

H.1.3
Independence

1.

Discrete Symbol Representation
is not derivable from finite-state control or universality alone. One could imagine a continuous control system without explicitly stipulating discrete symbols.

2.

Finite Control and State Transitions
cannot be inferred merely from having a discrete alphabet and the notion of a universal mechanism. One must explicitly stipulate
how
the machine progresses from one moment to the next in a finite-state manner.

3.

Algorithmic Universality
does not follow automatically from having discrete symbols and finite transitions. Without an explicit statement about the
generality
of these transitions, the system might be limited to a small class of tasks (not fully universal).

Hence, none of these axioms is derivable from the others, ensuring their mutual independence and collectively defining the foundations of classical digital computing.

H.2
Quantum Computing

Quantum computing extends classical digital computing by allowing information to be encoded in and processed by genuine quantum states. Below is a minimal set of axioms that capture the essence of quantum computation. Each axiom is independent of the others, and together they suffice to describe the behavior of all standard quantum computing models (circuit-based, measurement-based, adiabatic, etc.).

H.2.1
Axioms

1.

Quantum State Space (Hilbert Space).

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\psi\right>\;\in\;\mathcal{H},\hskip 9.24994pt\dim(\mathcal{H})<\infty,\hskip 9.24994pt\|\left|\psi\right>\|=1.

Statement.

Information is represented by vectors in a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
. Each
qubit
(or collection of qubits) is described by a normalized state
\left|\psi\right>
. Superposition and entanglement are direct consequences of the linear structure of
\mathcal{H}
.

2.

Unitary Evolution and Quantum Gates.

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\psi(t+\Delta t)\right>\;=\;\hat{U}\,\left|\psi(t)\right>,\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{U}=\hat{I}.

Statement.

In the absence of measurement, the evolution of quantum states is governed by
unitary
transformations
\hat{U}
. These can be discrete gates (e.g., single- or multi-qubit logic gates) or continuous evolution under a Hamiltonian
\hat{H}
(so
\hat{U}=e^{-\,i\,\hat{H}\,t/\hbar}
). Unitary operators preserve the norm and ensure that quantum computation is fundamentally reversible.

3.

Quantum Measurement Postulate.

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Outcomes}=\mathrm{Eigvals}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}),\hskip 9.24994ptp(\text{outcome})=\|\hat{P}_{\text{outcome}}\left|\psi\right>\|^{2}.

Statement.

Physical observables are represented by Hermitian operators
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
. Upon measurement, the system “collapses” (or is updated) to an eigenstate of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
with probability given by the Born rule. This underlies all qubit readout processes and more general measurement schemes (POVMs) in quantum computing.

H.2.2
Completeness

These three axioms suffice to describe the core structure and dynamics of quantum computers:

1.

Quantum State Space (Hilbert Space).

Formalizes the representation of quantum information, including superposition and entanglement.

2.

Unitary Evolution and Quantum Gates.

Describes how quantum information is processed or transformed, via discrete gates or continuous Hamiltonian evolution.

3.

Measurement Postulate.

Explains how classical outcomes emerge upon measurement and how the quantum state updates according to the measurement result (via projection or more general maps).

Together, these axioms enable universal quantum computation: one can construct quantum circuits, measurement-based algorithms, or adiabatic protocols capable of implementing arbitrary quantum algorithms.

H.2.3
Independence

1.

Quantum State Space
is not inferable from unitarity or measurement alone. One could posit abstract transformations and observations without specifying a complex Hilbert-space structure.

2.

Unitary Evolution
cannot be deduced purely from the existence of a Hilbert space and a measurement postulate. It must be explicitly stated that closed-system dynamics are governed by a norm-preserving (unitary) operation.

3.

Measurement Postulate
does not follow from the first two axioms. Even with a Hilbert space and unitary gates, one needs an additional rule specifying how measurement outcomes (eigenvalues) arise and how the post-measurement state is determined.

Hence, these three axioms are mutually independent while collectively forming the fundamental basis of quantum computing.

H.3
Transformation

H.3.1
Axiom 1

Goal:
Transform a subset of Digital Computing axioms

\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{3}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Discrete Symbol Representation},\\
A_{3}&:\text{Algorithmic Universality (Effective Computability)},\end{aligned}

into the
Quantum Computing
statement:

Q_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\psi\right>\in\mathcal{H},\;\|\left|\psi\right>\|=1,\;\dim(\mathcal{H})<\infty.

Here, we re-interpret the finite alphabet
\Sigma
(from
A_{1}
) as an orthonormal basis for a finite-dimensional Hilbert space.

Initial Set of Axioms (Digital, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{3}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Information is encoded in a finite alphabet }\Sigma,

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,\mathcal{U}:\;\forall\,f\in\mathcal{C},\;\exists\,p\in\Sigma^{*},\;\forall\,x\in\Sigma^{*}:\;\mathcal{U}(p,x)=f(x).

The discrete alphabet
\Sigma
must be explicitly carried along with
A_{3}
, ensuring that the universal machine manipulates finite-symbol data.

Rewrite Step 1: Replace Finite Alphabet
\Sigma
by Finite-Dimensional Basis.

A_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\Sigma\;\to\;\{\left|0\right>,\left|1\right>,\dots,\left|n-1\right>\}\subset\mathcal{H}.

Symbolically, the discrete symbols
\{0,1,\ldots\}
become orthonormal basis states
\{\left|j\right>\}
in a finite-dimensional Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
.
Hence:

S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{3}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\bigl\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{3}\bigr\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{3}\}}

where

A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Basis vectors in a finite-dimensional Hilbert space represent the discrete symbols},

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Universal computability over }\Sigma.

Rewrite Step 2: Allow Arbitrary Superpositions.

Though
A_{1}^{\prime}
introduced a basis for
\Sigma
, classical machines only occupy one symbol at a time. In the quantum model, however, we allow any normalized linear combination (superposition):

A_{1}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{1}^{\prime\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\psi\right>=\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}\,\left|j\right>,\hskip 9.24994pt\alpha_{j}\in\mathbb{C},\hskip 9.24994pt\|\left|\psi\right>\|=1.

Symbolically,

S_{1}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime\prime},\,A_{3}\}.

We still retain the universal mechanism idea from
A_{3}
, but now extended to the possibility of superposition states.

Rewrite Step 3: Conclude Quantum State Space (Axiom
Q_{1}
).

Given that
\Sigma
supplies a finite dimension, we define the full quantum state
\left|\psi\right>
in that Hilbert space. Thus,

Q_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\psi\right>\in\mathcal{H},\hskip 9.24994pt\|\left|\psi\right>\|=1,\hskip 9.24994pt\dim(\mathcal{H})<\infty.

Hence:

S_{2}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime\prime},\,A_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,Q_{1}\}.

Final Set (Quantum Axiom 1: State Space).

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{Q_{1}:\;\left|\psi\right>\in\mathcal{H},\;\|\left|\psi\right>\|=1,\;\dim(\mathcal{H})<\infty\Bigr\}.}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact):

\text{}\hskip 9.24994pt\underbrace{\{A_{1},A_{3}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{A_{1}^{\prime},A_{3}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{A_{1}^{\prime\prime},A_{3}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{Q_{1}\}}_{S_{3}}.

H.3.2
Axiom 2

Goal:
Starting from the essential Digital Axiom

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Finite Control and State Transitions}),

plus
the discrete assumption
A_{1}
that states and symbols lie in
\Sigma
, we want to derive

Q_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\psi(t+\Delta t)\right>=\hat{U}\,\left|\psi(t)\right>,\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{U}=\hat{I}.

That is, the classical update function
\delta
becomes a
unitary
map on the quantum state space.

Initial Set of Axioms:

S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\bigr\}

where

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\Sigma\text{ is finite (discrete symbols)},\hskip 9.24994ptA_{2}:\hskip 9.24994ptS\xrightarrow{\delta}S^{\prime},\;S,S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S},\;\mathcal{S}\text{ finite}.

We keep in mind that
\delta
handles
discrete
states.

Rewrite Step 1: Represent Internal States in a Hilbert Space.

We identify each classical state
S_{j}\in\mathcal{S}
with a basis vector
\left|S_{j}\right>
. Together with
\Sigma
, we have a product space for “internal state + symbol.” Symbolically,

A_{1},\,A_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;B_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|S_{j}\right>\otimes\left|x\right>\hskip 9.24994pt(x\in\Sigma).

S_{0}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{1}=\{\,B_{2}\}.

Rewrite Step 2: Generalize
\delta
to a Linear Operator.

Classically,
\delta
just picks the next state:
(S_{j},x)\mapsto S_{j^{\prime}}
. In the quantum model, we define a
linear
operator
\hat{U}
that acts on superpositions of
\left|S_{j}\right>\otimes\left|x\right>
.

B_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;B_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{U}:\;\left|S_{j}\right>\otimes\left|x\right>\;\mapsto\;\left|S_{j^{\prime}}\right>.

S_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{2}=\{\,B_{2}^{\prime}\}.

Rewrite Step 3: Impose Unitarity (Reversibility).

To maintain quantum consistency (norm preservation, reversibility),
\hat{U}
must be unitary:

\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{U}=\hat{I}.

Hence,

B_{2}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;Q_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\left|\psi(t+\Delta t)\right>=\hat{U}\,\left|\psi(t)\right>.

S_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{3}=\{\,Q_{2}\}.

Final Set (Quantum Axiom 2, Unitary Evolution):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{Q_{2}:\;\left|\psi(t+\Delta t)\right>=\hat{U}\,\left|\psi(t)\right>,\;\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{U}=\hat{I}\Bigr\}.}

\text{Symbolic Evolution (Compact):}\hskip 9.24994pt\underbrace{\{A_{1},A_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{B_{2}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{B_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{Q_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}.

H.3.3
Axiom 3

Goal:
Starting with the classical “algorithmic universality” axiom
A_{3}
(together with discrete symbols from
A_{1}
), we wish to derive the
Quantum Measurement Postulate
:

Q_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Outcomes}=\mathrm{Eigvals}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}),\hskip 9.24994ptp(\mathrm{outcome})=\|\hat{P}_{\mathrm{outcome}}\left|\psi\right>\|^{2}.

We will decompose the transition into smaller symbolic rewrites, showing exactly how “universal classical outputs” become “measurement outcomes” in quantum theory.

Initial Setup (Digital + Universality + Discrete Symbols):

\boxed{S_{0}=\{\,A_{1},\,A_{3}\}}\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Discrete Symbol Representation (alphabet }\Sigma\text{)},\\
A_{3}&:\text{Algorithmic Universality (over }\Sigma\text{)}.\end{aligned}

Here, the universal machine can compute any function
f:\Sigma^{*}\to\Sigma^{*}
and produce
classical
outputs in
\Sigma
. We aim to see how these outputs become quantum measurement outcomes.

Rewrite Step 1: Formalize an
Output Function

in the Classical Universal Machine.

In the classical setting, a universal machine
\mathcal{U}
takes input
(p,x)
(program
p
and data
x
), then halts with an
output

y\in\Sigma
. Symbolically,

A_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;O_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{U}(p,x)\;=\;y,\hskip 9.24994pty\in\Sigma.

Thus:

S_{0}=\{\,A_{1},\,A_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,O_{3}\}.

Interpretation:
We have made explicit that the classical machine must eventually yield a
discrete symbol

y\in\Sigma
. Before,
A_{3}
said “for each computable
f
, there is a program
p
so that
\mathcal{U}(p,x)=f(x)
.” Now we highlight the
\mathcal{U}\to y
output transition as
O_{3}
.

Rewrite Step 2: In Quantum Theory, Introduce a Hermitian Operator for Output.

To reproduce “classical outputs” in the quantum model, we define a Hermitian operator
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
whose eigenvalues
\{\lambda\}
label possible outcomes (classical symbols). Symbolically,

O_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;M_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\;\mathrm{Eigvals}(\hat{\mathcal{O}})=\{\lambda\}),

where each
\lambda
corresponds to a potential discrete output. Thus:

S_{1}=\{\,O_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,M_{3}\}.

Interpretation:
We replace the idea of “classical output
y\in\Sigma
” with “quantum measurement eigenvalue
\lambda
.” The operator
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
is the quantum mechanism that yields
\lambda
upon measurement.

Rewrite Step 3: Enforce the Born Rule for Probabilistic Outcomes.

Finally, to connect the machine’s quantum state
\left|\psi\right>
to a definite classical symbol
\lambda
, we impose:

p(\text{outcome}=\lambda)=\bigl\|\hat{P}_{\lambda}\left|\psi\right>\bigr\|^{2},

where
\hat{P}_{\lambda}
is the projector onto the eigenspace of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
with eigenvalue
\lambda
. Symbolically,

M_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;Q_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Measurement Postulate}).

Hence:

S_{2}=\{\,M_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,Q_{3}\}.

Interpretation:
We have now fully replaced “classical universal output” with the quantum notion of “measuring
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
, obtaining eigenvalue
\lambda
” with probability given by the Born rule.

Final Set (Quantum Axiom 3: Measurement).

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{Q_{3}:\;\hat{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\dagger},\;\mathrm{Outcomes}=\mathrm{Eigvals}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}),\;p(\mathrm{outcome})=\|\hat{P}_{\mathrm{outcome}}\left|\psi\right>\|^{2}\Bigr\}.}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{A_{1},\,A_{3}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{O_{3}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{M_{3}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{Q_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}.

H.3.4
Summary of Transformations (Digital
\to
Quantum)

1.

From Discrete Symbols (Axiom 1) to Quantum State Space (Q1).

We lift the finite alphabet
\Sigma
into a finite-dimensional Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
, permitting superpositions of basis states.

2.

From Finite Control (Axiom 2) to Unitary Evolution (Q2).

The classical transition function
\delta
generalizes to a linear, norm-preserving operator
\hat{U}
, ensuring reversibility of quantum evolution.

3.

From Algorithmic Universality (Axiom 3) to Measurement Postulate (Q3).

Classical outputs must be read out from quantum states, so we formalize observables via Hermitian operators and outcome probabilities via the Born rule.

Together, these steps illustrate how each digital axiom—
including the discrete representation from
A_{1}
—carries forward
and is reinterpreted in the quantum framework, yielding the three quantum axioms of state space, unitary evolution, and measurement.

Appendix I
From Classical/Newtonian Physics to General Relativity Theory

I.1
Classical/Newtonian Physics

Newtonian physics treats spacetime as flat and time as universal. It can be used to predict the motion of bodies experiencing forces and accelerations, as well as quantitatively describe gravity and calculate orbits of celestial bodies. It can only be successfully applied to objects moving at nonrelativistic velocities.

I.1.1
Axioms

1.

Absolute Space and Time

\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},\,ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

Where
dt_{i}
and
dx_{i},dy_{i},dz_{i}
denote the time separation of two events, as measured in frame
i
. In other words, space is three-dimensional, Euclidean, and absolute; time flows uniformly and is the same for all observers.

2.

Existence of Inertial Frames

\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

There exist reference frames (inertial frames) in which Newton’s laws of motion hold true without modification.

3.

Newton’s Second Law (Law of Acceleration)

\vec{F}=m\vec{a}=m\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

In an inertial frame, the net external force acting on a body equals the rate of change of its momentum.

4.

Newton’s Third Law (Law of Action and Reaction)

\vec{F}=-\vec{R}

For every action
\vec{F}
, there is an equal and opposite reaction
\vec{R}
; forces between two bodies are equal in magnitude and opposite in direction.

5.

Principle of Superposition

\vec{F}=\sum_{i}\vec{F_{i}}

When multiple forces
\vec{F_{i}}
act on a body, the net force
\vec{F}
is the vector sum of the individual forces acting on it.

6.

Law of Universal Gravitation

\vec{F_{g}}=\frac{Gm_{1}m_{2}}{r^{2}}\hat{r}

Every point mass
2
attracts every other point mass
1
with a force along the unit vector
\hat{r}
pointing from
1
to
2
, proportional to the product of their masses
m_{1},m_{2},
and inversely proportional to the square of the
r
distance between them.

I.1.2
Completeness

1.

Absolute Space and Time
(
Axiom 1
) shows the mathematical structure of system parameters and how they can be transformed between reference frames.

2.

Existence of Inertial Frames
(
Axiom 2
) shows that Newton’s laws can be applied to our universe.

3.

Newton’s Second Law
(
Axiom 3
),
Newton’s Third Law
(
Axiom 4
), and
Principle of Superposition
(
Axiom 5
) give a way of predicting a body’s motion, provided forces acting on it.

4.

Law of Universal Gravitation
(
Axiom 6
) describes the fundamental force of gravity, which attracts any two objects.

From these axioms, one can predict the motion of celestial objects, transform between reference frames, draw force diagrams, and model the behavior of arbitrary systems (for instance rotation), given the forces. They suffice to describe the basic phenomena of Newtonian mechanics.

I.1.3
Independence

1.

Absolute Space and Time
sets the mathematical framework to which other other axioms should be applied.

2.

Existence of Inertial Frames
can’t be derived from other axioms, which explicitly assume being in an inertial reference frame.

3.

Newton’s Second Law
is the only axiom that quantitatively describes the effect of an arbitrary force on a body’s motion.

4.

Newton’s Third Law
is the only axiom that refers to an arbitrary interaction between two bodies.

5.

Principle of Superposition
is the only action that demonstrates the effects of multiple forces on a body.

6.

Law of Universal Gravitation
refers to gravity, a concept not introduced in previous axioms.

I.2
General relativity

The fundamental difference between general relativity and Newtonian mechanics is the assumption that the speed of light is the same in all reference frames. This, and a careful analysis of the phenomenon of gravity, leads to the description of spacetime as curved, with no universal notion of Euclidean length or time. The theory has been able to predict the existence of black holes and the bending of light by massive objects and accurately describe orbits in strong gravity conditions. General relativity has great practical usage as well, for instance, to accurately program GPS systems.

I.2.1
Axioms

1.

Principle of Equivalence (Einstein’s Equivalence Principle)

Gravity indistinguishable from acceleration

Locally, the effects of gravity are indistinguishable from those of acceleration. This means that in a small enough region of spacetime, the laws of physics reduce to those of Special Relativity, and gravitational effects can be transformed away by choosing an appropriate accelerated reference frame.

2.

Principle of General Covariance

g_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\prime\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\prime\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\text{, }\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})

Where primed
x^{\prime\mu}
and unprimed
x^{\mu}
coordinates correspond to different reference frames.
g_{\mu\nu}
and
g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}
are the corresponding metrics, and
\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})
corresponds to the Lagrangian density in different reference frames.

In other words, the laws of physics are formulated in a way that is valid under any smooth coordinate transformations. This means that the fundamental equations are tensor equations, ensuring they hold true in all coordinate systems.

3.

Spacetime as a Four-Dimensional Pseudo-Riemannian Manifold

(M_{4},g_{\mu\nu})\text{ with signature }(-,+,+,+)

Spacetime is modeled as a smooth, four-dimensional manifold
M_{4}
equipped with a metric tensor
g_{\mu\nu}
of Lorentzian signature
(-,+,+,+)
. The metric tensor defines distances and angles in spacetime and determines its geometric and causal structure.

4.

Einstein’s Field Equations

R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}

Where
R_{\mu\nu}
is the Ricci curvature tensor,
g_{\mu\nu}
is the metric tensor,
R
is the Ricci scalar (trace of the Ricci tensor),
\Lambda
is the cosmological constant,
G
is the gravitational constant, and
c
is the speed of light.

The curvature of spacetime is directly related to the energy and momentum content within it.

5.

Geodesic Principle (Motion of Free Particles)

\forall\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow\nabla_{\tau}v=0,\hskip 9.24994ptv^{\mu}=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\tau}

Where
\nabla_{\tau}
denotes the covariant derivative, and
\tau
the proper time.
In the absence of non-gravitational forces
\vec{F}
, free-falling test particles move along timelike geodesics of spacetime.

6.

Metric Compatibility and Torsion-Free Connection (Levi-Civita Connection)

{\Delta}_{\lambda}g_{\mu\nu}=0\text{, }{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}={\Gamma}_{\nu\mu}^{\lambda}

The connection on the spacetime manifold is the unique torsion-free, metric-compatible Levi-Civita connection.

I.2.2
Completeness

1.

Principle of Equivalence
(
Axiom 1
) allows one to connect general relativity to special relativity to derive the geodesics equation and predict the nature of spacetime.

2.

Principle of General Covariance
(
Axiom 2
),
Spacetime as a Four-Dimensional Pseudo-Riemannian Manifold
(
Axiom 3
), and
Metric Compatibility and Torsion-Free Connection (Levi-Civita Connection)
(
Axiom 6
) lay the basis of a mathematical framework that should describe reality.

3.

Einstein’s Field Equations
(
Axiom 4
) quantitatively relate the curvature of spacetime to present matter. It introduces the empirical gravitational constant, which lets one describe the expansion of the universe.

4.

The geodesic principle
(
Axiom 5
) describes the motion of free particles.

Together, these axioms let one describe the universe as curved spacetime and allow for a quantitative description of various phenomena, such as black holes, gravitational waves, and gravitational lensing.

I.2.3
Independence

1.

Principle of Equivalence
cannot be derived from other axioms and establishes the nature of gravity.

2.

Principle of General Covariance
is the only universal statement about physical laws in the general relativity framework that is crucial for deriving them.

3.

Spacetime as a Four-Dimensional Pseudo-Riemannian Manifold
is essential to introduce the notion of a metric and curvature to describe spacetime.

4.

Einstein’s Field Equations
contain the empirical cosmological constant, which can’t be evaluated from other axioms.

5.

The geodesic principle
is the only equivalent of Newton’s laws that lets one predict the motion of a free particle.

6.

Metric Compatibility and Torsion-Free Connection
is an assumption about the Riemannian manifold that needs to be imposed and is not explicitly introduced by other axioms.

I.3
Transformation

I.3.1
Axiom 1

Goal:
Transform a subset of Newtonian axioms

\bigl\{\ A_{3},A_{5}\bigr\}\ \hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{3}&:\text{Newton's second law},\\
A_{5}&:\text{Law of Universal Gravitation},\end{aligned}

into a
General Relativity
statement:

W_{1}:\text{Gravity indistinguishable from acceleration}

All intermediate steps are small symbolic rewrites that gradually evolve the original set into the final one.

Initial Set of Axioms (Newtonian mechanics, subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{3},A_{5}\bigr\}}

where

A_{3}:\vec{F}=m\vec{a}=m\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}\hskip 18.49988ptA_{5}:\vec{F_{g}}=\frac{Gm_{1}m_{2}}{r^{2}}\hat{r}

Rewrite Step 1: calculate acceleration due to gravity

\bigl\{A_{3},A_{5}\bigr\}\longrightarrow A_{5}^{\prime\prime}:\vec{a_{g}}=\frac{\vec{F_{g}}}{m_{1}}=\frac{Gm_{2}}{r^{2}}

Newton’s second law lets one calculate acceleration that a body experiences due to gravity as

\vec{a_{g}}=\frac{\vec{F_{g}}}{m_{1}}=\frac{Gm_{2}}{r^{2}}

S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{3},A_{5}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{1}=\bigl\{W_{1}^{\prime}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\bigl\{W_{1}^{\prime}\bigr\}}

where

W_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{a_{g}}=\frac{\vec{F_{g}}}{m_{1}}=\frac{Gm_{2}}{r^{2}}

Rewrite Step 2: obtaining the equivalence principle

W_{1}^{\prime}\longrightarrow W_{1}:\text{gravity indistinguishable from acceleration}

Due to
W_{1}^{\prime}
, the gravity produces acceleration. The same acceleration could have been produced by other forces, or the fact of being in a non-inertial reference frame. Hence, one arrives at the principle of equivalence, which states that the effects of gravity are locally indistinguishable from acceleration:

S_{1}=\bigl\{W_{1}^{\prime}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{2}=\bigl\{\,W_{1}\bigr\}

Final Set (Principle of Equivalence):

\boxed{S_{2}=\Bigl\{W_{1}:\;\text{gravity indistinguishable from acceleration}\Bigr\}}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact View):

\underbrace{\{\ A_{3},A_{5}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{1}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{1}\}}_{S_{2}}

I.3.2
Axiom 2

Goal:
Transform a subset of Newtonian axioms

\bigl\{\ A_{1},A_{2}\bigr\}\ \hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Absolute Space and Time},\\
A_{2}&:\text{Existence of Inertial Frames},\end{aligned}

into a
General Relativity
statement:

W_{2}:g_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\prime\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\prime\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\text{, }\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})

All intermediate steps are small symbolic rewrites that gradually evolve the original set into the final one.

Initial Set of Axioms (Newtonian mechanics, subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{1},A_{2}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},\,ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2},

A_{2}:\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Rewrite Step 1: modify the concept of Euclidean space to a unified 4-dimensional spacetime

A_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{1}^{\prime}

A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\text{ Frames 1, 2: }x_{i}^{\mu}=(ct_{i},x_{i},y_{i},z_{i})\text{, and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=-cdt_{i}^{2}+dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

Symbolically, we replace

\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},\,ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2},\,dt_{1}=dt_{2}

by

\forall\text{ Frames 1, 2: }x_{i}^{\mu}=(ct_{i},x_{i},y_{i},z_{i})\text{, and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=-cdt_{i}^{2}+dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{1}=\bigl\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\bigl\{A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}^{\prime}:\text{4-dimensional spacetime}\hskip 18.49988ptA_{2}:\text{Existence of Inertial Frames}

Rewrite Step 2: introduce the Lorenz transformations

A_{1}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{1}^{\prime\prime}:\hskip 9.24994ptx^{\prime\mu}={\Lambda}_{\nu}^{\mu}x^{\nu},\eta^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}

Where primed and unprimed coordinates correspond to different reference frames. From the invariance of the spacetime interval
ds^{2}
one can derive Lorenz transformations and the metric
\eta_{\mu\nu}=\text{diag}(-1,1,1,1)
is the same in all inertial reference frames.

Symbolically, we replace

\forall\text{ Frames 1, 2: }x_{i}^{\mu}=(ct_{i},x_{i},y_{i},z_{i})\text{, and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=-cdt_{i}^{2}+dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

by

\hskip 9.24994ptx^{\prime\mu}={\Lambda}_{\nu}^{\mu}x^{\nu},\eta^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}

S_{1}=\bigl\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{2}=\bigl\{\,A_{1}^{\prime\prime},\,A_{2}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\bigl\{A_{1}^{\prime\prime},A_{2}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}^{\prime\prime}:x^{\prime\mu}={\Lambda}_{\nu}^{\mu}x^{\nu},\eta^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}\hskip 18.49988ptA_{2}:\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Rewrite Step 3: introduce general coordinate transformations

\bigl\{A_{1}^{\prime\prime},A_{2}\bigr\}\longrightarrow{W_{1}}

A_{1}^{\prime\prime}
applies to inertial frames (which exist by
A_{2}
). Generalize the transformation between reference frames
x^{\prime\mu}={\Lambda}_{\nu}^{\mu}x^{\nu}
to a general transformation
x^{\mu}\longrightarrow x^{\prime\mu}
and transform the metric accordingly:

g_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\prime\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\prime\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\text{, }

Additionally, assume the invariance of physical laws under such transformations, expressed by the Lagrangian density:

\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})

Hence:

S_{2}=\bigl\{A_{1}^{\prime\prime},A_{2}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{3}=\bigl\{W_{2}\bigr\}

where

W_{2}:\hskip 9.24994ptg_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\prime\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\prime\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\text{, }\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})

Final Set (General relativity Axiom 2, Principle of General Covariance):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{W_{2}:\;g_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\prime\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\prime\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\text{, }\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact View):

\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime\prime},A_{2}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}

I.3.3
Axiom 3

Goal:
Transform a subset of Newtonian axioms

\bigl\{\ A_{1},A_{2}\bigr\}\ \hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Absolute Space and Time},\\
A_{2}&:\text{Existence of Inertial Frames},\end{aligned}

into a
General Relativity
statement:

W_{3}:(M_{4},g_{\mu\nu})\text{ with signature }(-,+,+,+)

All intermediate steps are small symbolic rewrites that gradually evolve the original set into the final one.

Initial Set of Axioms (Newtonian mechanics, subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{1},A_{2}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

A_{2}:\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv_{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Rewrite Step 1: modify the concept of Euclidean space to a unified 4-dimensional spacetime

A_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{1}^{\prime}

A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\text{ Frames 1, 2: }x_{i}^{\mu}=(ct_{i},x_{i},y_{i},z_{i})\text{, and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=-cdt_{i}^{2}+dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

Symbolically, we replace

\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

by

\forall\text{ Frames 1, 2: }x_{i}^{\mu}=(ct_{i},x_{i},y_{i},z_{i})\text{, and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=-cdt_{i}^{2}+dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{1}=\bigl\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\bigl\{A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}^{\prime}:\forall\text{ Frames 1, 2: }x_{i}^{\mu}=(ct_{i},x_{i},y_{i},z_{i})\text{, and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=-cdt_{i}^{2}+dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

A_{2}:\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Rewrite Step 2: generalizing the metric

\bigl\{A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}\bigr\}\longrightarrow W_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994ptx^{\mu}=(ct,x,y,z)\text{, and metric }ds^{2}=g_{\mu\nu}dx^{\mu}dx^{\nu}

From axiom
A_{1}^{\prime}
, one gets a special metric
\eta_{\mu\nu}=\text{diag}(-1,1,1,1)
for inertial reference frames (which exist by Axiom 2). It can be generalized;

ds^{2}=g_{\mu\nu}dx^{\mu}dx^{\nu}

Hence

S_{1}=\bigl\{\,A_{1}^{\prime},\,A_{2}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{2}=\bigl\{\,W_{3}^{\prime}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\bigl\{W_{3}^{\prime}\bigr\}}

W_{3}^{\prime}\longrightarrow W_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt(M_{4},g_{\mu\nu})\text{ with signature }(-,+,+,+)

By letting
g_{\mu\nu}
vary, one gets curved 4-dimensional spacetime which can be modeled as a 4-dimensional pseudo-Riemannian manifold.

S_{2}=\bigl\{W_{3}^{\prime}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{3}=\bigl\{W_{3}\bigr\}

Final Set (General relativity Axiom 3, Spacetime as a Four-Dimensional Pseudo-Riemannian Manifold):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{W_{3}:\;(M_{4},g_{\mu\nu})\text{ with signature }(-,+,+,+)\Bigr\}}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact View):

\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}

Axiom 4

Goal:
Transform a subset of Newtonian axioms

\bigl\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{5}\bigr\}\ \hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Absolute Space and Time},\\

where

All intermediate steps are small symbolic rewrites that gradually evolve the original set into the final one.

Initial Set of Axioms (Newtonian mechanics, subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{1},A_{2},A_{5}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},\,ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2},

A_{5}:\vec{F_{g}}=\frac{Gm_{1}m_{2}}{r^{2}}\hat{r}

Rewrite Steps 1-3: introducing the Principle of General Covariance

\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime\prime},A_{2}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}

These rewrite steps are the same ones that lead to axiom 2 (
A_{5}
stays unchanged). Hence, for brevity, they were not repeated here in detail.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{3}=\bigl\{W_{2},A_{5}\bigr\}}

where

W_{2}:g_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\prime\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\prime\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\text{, }\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})

A_{5}:\vec{F_{g}}=\frac{Gm_{1}m_{2}}{r^{2}}\hat{r}

Rewrite Step 4: Poisson’s Equation for Gravity

A_{5}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{5}^{\prime}

A_{5}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt{\nabla}^{2}\phi=4\pi G\rho

In other words, the law of universal gravitation

A_{5}:\vec{F_{g}}=\frac{Gm_{1}m_{2}}{r^{2}}\hat{r}

gets rewritten in the equivalent form of a differential equation, where
\phi
is the gravitational potential, and
\rho
is matter density.

S_{3}=\bigl\{\,W_{3},\,A_{5}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{4}=\bigl\{\,W_{3},\,A_{5}^{\prime}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{4}=\bigl\{W_{3},A_{5}^{\prime}\bigr\}}

where

W_{2}:g_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\alpha}}{\partial x^{\prime\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\beta}}{\partial x^{\prime\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta}(x)\text{, }\mathcal{L}(x^{\mu},g_{\mu\nu})=\mathcal{L^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\mu},g^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})

A_{5}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt{\nabla}^{2}\phi=4\pi G\rho

Rewrite Step 5: Introduce Einstein’s equations

\bigl\{W_{2},\;A_{5}\bigr\}\;\longrightarrow W_{4}^{\prime}:R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}

Due to
W_{2}
, the law of gravity:

A_{5}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt{\nabla}^{2}\phi=4\pi G\rho

should be modified to a covariant form. The tensor equivalent of matter density
\rho
is the stress-energy tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
. In curved spacetime, the term associated with gravitational potential
\nabla^{2}\phi
can be replaced with
R_{\mu\nu}-(1/2)g_{\mu\nu}R
. The constant on the right-hand side can be chosen to accurately describe the Newtonian limit. Finally, one arrives at:

W_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994ptR_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}

S_{4}=\bigl\{\,W_{3},\,A_{5}^{\prime}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{5}=\bigl\{\,W_{4}^{\prime}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{5}=\bigl\{W_{4}^{\prime}\bigr\}}

where

W_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994ptR_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}

Rewrite Step 6: adding the cosmological constant

W_{4}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{4}:R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}

To properly describe the expansion of the universe and consider dark energy, the equation

W_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994ptR_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}

gets turned into

W_{4}:R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu},

where
\Lambda
is the cosmological constant.

S_{5}=\bigl\{W_{4}^{\prime}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{6}=\bigl\{W_{4}\bigr\}

Final Set (Einstein’s field equations):

\boxed{S_{6}=\Bigl\{W_{4}:\;R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact View):

\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{5}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2},A_{5}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime\prime},A_{2},A_{5}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{2},A_{5}\}}_{S_{3}}\;\longrightarrow\;

\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{2},A_{5}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{4}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{4}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{5}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{4}\}}_{S_{6}}

I.3.5
Axiom 5

Goal:
Transform a subset of Newtonian axioms

\bigl\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\bigr\}\ \hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Absolute Space and Time},\\
A_{2}&:\text{Existence of Inertial Frames},\\
A_{3}&:\text{Newton's second law}\end{aligned}

into a
General Relativity
statement:

W_{5}:\forall\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow\nabla_{\tau}v=0,\hskip 9.24994ptv^{\mu}=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\tau}

All intermediate steps are small symbolic rewrites that gradually evolve the original set into the final one.

Initial Set of Axioms (Newtonian mechanics, subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{F}=m\vec{a}=m\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Rewrite Step 1: introduce Newton’s first law

A_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{3}^{\prime}:\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=0,\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Symbolically, by setting
\vec{F}=0
in

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{F}=m\vec{a}=m\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}

one gets

A_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=0

S_{0}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2},A_{3}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{1}=\bigl\{\,A_{1},\,A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\bigr\}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\bigl\{A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

A_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=0

Rewrite Steps 2-4: spacetime as a four-dimensional pseudo-Riemannian manifold

\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3}^{\prime},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{3}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{4}}

These rewrite steps are the same ones that lead to axiom 3. Hence, for brevity, they were not repeated here in detail.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{4}=\bigl\{W_{3},A_{3}^{\prime}\bigr\}}

where

W_{3}:(M_{4},g_{\mu\nu})\text{ with signature }(-,+,+,+),\hskip 18.49988ptA_{3}^{\prime}:\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=0,\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Rewrite Step 5: introduce the geodesic principle

\bigl\{W_{3},A_{3}^{\prime}\bigr\}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{5}:\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow 0={\nabla}_{\tau}v^{\lambda}=\frac{dv^{\lambda}}{d\tau}+{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}v^{\mu}v^{\nu},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{\mu}=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\tau}

Because in curved spacetime, the notion of absolute time does not exist, replace
t
with proper time
\tau
- time as measured by the object. Moreover, since spacetime is four dimensional, velocity v is now a 4-vector (
0\leq\mu\leq 3
as opposed to
1\leq i\leq 3
).

\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}\;\longrightarrow\;\hskip 9.24994ptv^{\mu}=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\tau}

Moreover, to account for the curvature of spacetime (described by
W_{3}
), a covariant derivative
\nabla_{\tau}
instead of the normal time derivative
d/d\tau
is introduced.
\nabla_{\tau}
includes a correction expressed by Christoffel symbols
{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}
, which describe how the basis vectors
e_{\nu}
change as one moves through spacetime;

\nabla_{\mu}e_{\nu}={\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}e_{\lambda}

Hence,

A_{3}^{\prime}:\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=0,\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

gets transformed into

W_{5}:\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow 0={\nabla}_{\tau}v^{\lambda}=\frac{dv^{\lambda}}{d\tau}+{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}v^{\mu}v^{\nu},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{\mu}=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\tau}

Final Set (Geodesic Principle):

\boxed{S_{6}=\Bigl\{W_{5}:\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow 0={\nabla}_{\tau}v^{\lambda}=\frac{dv^{\lambda}}{d\tau}+{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}v^{\mu}v^{\nu},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{\mu}=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\tau}\Bigr\}}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact View):

\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3}^{\prime},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{3}}\;\longrightarrow\;

\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{4}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{5}\}}_{S_{5}}

Axiom 6

Goal:
Transform a subset of Newtonian axioms

\bigl\{A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{aligned} A_{1}&:\text{Absolute Space and Time},\\
A_{2}&:\text{Existence of Inertial Frames},\\
A_{3}&:\text{Newton's second law}\end{aligned}

into a
General Relativity
statement:

W_{6}:{\Delta}_{\lambda}g_{\mu\nu}=0\text{, }{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}={\Gamma}_{\nu\mu}^{\lambda}

All intermediate steps are small symbolic rewrites that gradually evolve the original set into the final one.

Initial Set of Axioms (Newtonian mechanics, subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\bigr\}}

where

A_{1}:\forall\text{ Frames }1,2:dt_{1}=dt_{2},r_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}),\text{ and metric }ds_{i}^{2}=dx_{i}^{2}+dy_{i}^{2}+dz_{i}^{2},ds_{1}^{2}=ds_{2}^{2}

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\text{ Frame in which }\vec{F}=\vec{0}\Rightarrow\ \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt}=\vec{0},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{F}=m\vec{a}=m\frac{d\vec{v}}{dt},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{i}=\frac{dx^{i}}{dt}

Rewrite Steps 1-5: introduce the geodesic principle and equation

\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ A_{1}^{\prime},A_{2},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3}^{\prime},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{3}}\;\longrightarrow\;

\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{3},A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{4}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{\ W_{5}\}}_{S_{5}}

These rewrite steps are the same ones that lead to axiom 5. Hence, for brevity, they were not repeated here in detail.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{5}=\bigl\{W_{5}\bigr\}}

where

W_{5}:\vec{F}=0\Rightarrow 0={\nabla}_{\tau}v^{\lambda}=\frac{dv^{\lambda}}{d\tau}+{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}v^{\mu}v^{\nu},\hskip 9.24994ptv^{\mu}=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\tau}

Rewrite Step 6: introducing metric compatibility
and torsion-free connection

W_{5}\;\longrightarrow\;W_{6}:{\nabla}_{\lambda}g_{\mu\nu}=0,\hskip 9.24994pt{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}={\Gamma}_{\nu\mu}^{\lambda}

The fact that the connection represented by the Christoffel symbols is torsion-free should be introduced as an additional assumption;

{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}={\Gamma}_{\nu\mu}^{\lambda}

In such case, the Christoffel symbols can be expressed as

{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}g^{\lambda\rho}\Big(\frac{\partial g_{\mu\rho}}{\partial x^{\nu}}+\frac{\partial g_{\rho\nu}}{\partial x^{\mu}}-\frac{\partial g_{\nu\mu}}{\partial x^{\rho}}\Big)

From this and the definition of the covariant derivative it follows that

{\nabla}_{\lambda}g_{\mu\nu}=0

Therefore,

S_{5}=\bigl\{\,W_{5}\bigr\}\longrightarrow S_{6}=\bigl\{\,W_{6}\bigr\}

Final Set (Metric Compatibility and Torsion-Free Connection):
