Symbolically, strictly Earth-centered orbits lead to increasingly cumbersome constructions of deferents and epicycles, especially for outer planets, to fit observed variations in apparent speed and direction.

S_{0}=\{\,G_{1}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,G_{1}^{\prime}\}.

G_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{The Ptolemaic system requires multiple epicycles for accurate predictions,}

\text{raising theoretical complexity}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,G_{1}^{\prime}\}}

G_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{excessive epicycles in geocentric orbits hint at a simpler underlying geometry}).

Rewrite Step 2: Note Earth’s Similarities to Other Planets.

G_{1}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{1}^{*},

where

H_{1}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Earth shares observational patterns with Venus, Mars, Jupiter, etc.}),\\

(\text{phases, retrograde illusions, orbits about }\text{some central luminary}).

Symbolically, Copernican insight: Earth and other planets exhibit analogous complexities, suggesting Earth is a planet too.
Hence, it is more natural to treat the Sun—not Earth—as the pivot for planetary motion.

S_{1}=\{\,G_{1}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,H_{1}^{*}\}.

H_{1}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Earth is not unique; it's plausibly one among the \textquotedblleft wanderers\textquotedblright}

orbiting a brighter central body (Sun).

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,H_{1}^{*}\}}

H_{1}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{conceptual shift: Earth behaves similarly to other known planets}).

Rewrite Step 3: Place the Sun at Orbital Center for All Planets.

Finally, we state the heliocentric principle:

H_{1}^{*}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{1},

where

H_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,P\neq\text{Sun}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{CenterOrbit}(P)=\text{Sun}.

Symbolically, this replaces “Earth is the orbital center” with “The Sun is the orbital center of all planets, Earth included,” drastically simplifying the model for retrograde motion and epicycle usage.

S_{2}=\{\,H_{1}^{*}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,H_{1}\}.

H_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Heliocentric Axiom 1: The Sun, not Earth, is the center of planetary orbits.}

Final Set (Heliocentric Axiom 1: Central Sun):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{H_{1}:\ \forall\,P\neq\text{Sun}:\ \mathrm{CenterOrbit}(P)=\text{Sun}\Bigr\}.}

Thus, from the geocentric idea (Earth at center) plus observational complexities and the realization that Earth shares planetary traits, we end up with the Sun-centered concept that unifies planetary orbits more elegantly.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,G_{1}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{1}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{1}^{*}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{1}\}}_{S_{3}}.

Interpretation of Each Step:

1.

Excess epicycles
in a purely Earth-centered system suggest an overly complex framework.

2.

Earth’s behavior
is akin to other planets, motivating the notion that Earth, too, orbits a central luminary.

3.

Sun at center
simplifies orbits (especially retrograde explanation), yielding Copernicus’s heliocentric principle.

Hence, Geocentric Axiom 1 (Earth at center) transforms into Heliocentric Axiom 1 (Sun at center) once one reinterprets Earth as simply another planet.

Q.3.2

Axiom 2

Goal:
Transform the
Geocentric Axiom 2

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,X:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Orbit}(X)=\sum(\text{UniformCircles}),

into the
Heliocentric Axiom 2

H_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\;\wedge\;\mathrm{Revolution}(\mathrm{Earth},T_{\oplus}),

which declares that Earth both rotates on its axis (daily cycle) and revolves around the Sun (annual cycle).
Symbolically, we show how the purely
object-by-object
circular motion scheme of geocentrism evolves into a
dual motion
for Earth itself, eliminating the need for daily rotation of the entire heavens.

Initial Axiom (Geocentric, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{G_{2}\bigr\}}

where

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,X:\ \mathrm{Orbit}(X)=\sum(\text{UniformCircles}).

In Ptolemaic astronomy, each celestial body
X
follows a combination of perfect circles (deferent + epicycles), all at constant angular speed, to explain daily and seasonal motions.

Rewrite Step 1: Separate Stellar
Daily
Motion from Planetary Motions.

G_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{2}^{\prime},

where

G_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{the star-sphere rotates once daily}),

(\text{planets have additional slower circles for their wanderings}).

Symbolically, we partition uniform circular motion into:

\text{Star-sphere daily rotation}\hskip 9.24994pt\wedge\hskip 9.24994pt\text{planetary (incl.\ Sun, Moon) epicycles and slow orbits.}

Hence,
X
might revolve daily with the star-sphere, plus further circles for its own motion.

S_{0}=\{\,G_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,G_{2}^{\prime}\}.

G_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{One uniform circle for the entire heavens (daily), plus distinct epicycles for planets.}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,G_{2}^{\prime}\}}

G_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{massive star-sphere circle + slower orbits for Sun, Moon, planets}).

Rewrite Step 2: Reinterpret Daily Motion as Earth’s Rotation (Rather Than Star-Sphere).

G_{2}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{2}^{*},

where

H_{2}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{the daily cycle arises from Earth rotating once every 24 hours, }\\

\hskip 9.24994pt\text{rather than the entire celestial sphere spinning}).

Symbolically, we remove the star-sphere’s daily uniform rotation and assign it to Earth’s spin. This is a key Copernican shift: Instead of everything else turning once a day, Earth itself spins, explaining the apparent diurnal motion of the sky.

S_{1}=\{\,G_{2}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,H_{2}^{*}\}.

H_{2}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\implies\text{stars\textquoteright daily cycle is an illusion of Earth\textquoteright s spin}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,H_{2}^{*}\}}

H_{2}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{the daily uniform circle is ascribed to Earth\textquoteright s axis rotation}).

Rewrite Step 3: Add Earth’s Annual Revolution Around the Sun.

Finally, we incorporate the second major motion of Earth:

H_{2}^{*}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{2},

where

H_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\;\wedge\;\mathrm{Revolution}(\mathrm{Earth},T_{\oplus}\approx 1\text{ year}).

Symbolically, Earth is now singled out to have two distinct motions: a daily spin on its axis and an orbital revolution around the Sun. This accounts for seasonal changes, the Sun’s apparent yearly motion among the stars, and so forth.

S_{2}=\{\,H_{2}^{*}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,H_{2}\}.

H_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Heliocentric Axiom 2: Earth rotates once per day and orbits the Sun annually}.

Final Set (Heliocentric Axiom 2: Earth’s Dual Motion):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{H_{2}:\ \mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\;\wedge\;\mathrm{Revolution}(\mathrm{Earth},T_{\oplus})\Bigr\}.}

Thus, from the purely object-centered uniform circles in geocentrism (including a daily star-sphere spin), we shift to a system where Earth itself executes a daily rotation and an annual revolution, reconciling the same observed motions in a simpler scheme.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,G_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{2}^{*}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}.

Interpretation of Each Step:

1.

Uniform Circular Motion
(geocentrism) includes a daily star-sphere plus epicycles for planets.

2.

Assign daily rotation to Earth
instead of the entire star-sphere, simplifying the model drastically.

3.

Add Earth’s annual revolution
around the Sun, forming the twofold motion central to heliocentrism.

Hence, Geocentric Axiom 2 (all bodies follow sums of uniform circles around Earth, including a daily star-sphere) is replaced by Heliocentric Axiom 2 (Earth rotates daily
and
revolves yearly around the Sun).

Q.3.3

Axiom 3

Goal:
Transform the
Geocentric Axiom 3

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S_{\star}:\ (\text{Sphere of Fixed Stars}),

into the
Heliocentric Axiom 3

H_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Orbit}(P)=\sum(\text{UniformCircles}),

(i.e. the
Copernican Ideal
that each planet’s orbit around the Sun is a combination of uniform circular motions).
Symbolically, we show how the geocentric concept of a single star-sphere rotating around Earth is replaced by the idea that each planet (including Earth) follows an (approximately) circular orbit around the Sun, while the stars remain vastly distant.

Initial Axiom (Geocentric, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{G_{3}\bigr\}}

where

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S_{\star}:\ (\text{all stars on a single rotating sphere of fixed radius from Earth}).

This states that Earth is at the center of a great celestial sphere to which all stars are affixed.
Its rotation around Earth explains daily rising and setting of stars in the Ptolemaic view.

Rewrite Step 1: Attribute Star Motions to Earth’s Spin, Minimizing the Role of a Star-Sphere.

G_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{3}^{\prime},

where

G_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{the daily appearance of star rotation is due to Earth\textquoteright s rotation,}

\text{not an actual rotating star-sphere}).

Symbolically, we no longer need a physically rotating sphere of stars close to Earth; instead, we interpret the apparent stellar motion as a perspective effect from Earth’s 24-hour spin (as introduced in
Axiom 2 transformation
).

S_{0}=\{\,G_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,G_{3}^{\prime}\}.

G_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{the \textquotedblleft sphere of stars\textquotedblright is effectively stationary and extremely distant,}\\

\text{with Earth's rotation producing daily star paths across the sky}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,G_{3}^{\prime}\}}

G_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{no actual rotating shell needed; Earth\textquoteright s spin explains star positions\textquoteright daily arcs}).

Rewrite Step 2: Shift Focus from a Single Star-Sphere to Planetary Orbits Around the Sun.

G_{3}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{3}^{*},

where

H_{3}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{(Planets, including Earth, revolve around the Sun in some geometric system---}

possibly circles + epicycles).

Symbolically, once we no longer require all celestial objects (stars
and
planets) to share a common Earth-centered sphere, attention goes to the geometry of planetary orbits about the Sun. That is,
the star-sphere concept
becomes less central to explaining planetary motion; instead,
the Sun-planet orbital framework
takes precedence.

S_{1}=\{\,G_{3}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,H_{3}^{*}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,H_{3}^{*}\}}

H_{3}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Planets orbit the Sun in uniform circles, epicycles, or otherwise}).

Rewrite Step 3: Assert the Copernican Ideal of Uniform Circular Orbits Around the Sun.

Finally, we formulate the
Heliocentric Axiom 3
:

H_{3}^{*}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{3},

where

H_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\mathrm{planet}\ P):\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Orbit}(P)=\sum(\text{UniformCircles}),

i.e. each planet’s motion is composed of one or more perfect, uniformly traversed circles around the Sun.
In Copernicus’s original scheme, epicycles remain but are far fewer and simpler than the Ptolemaic version, primarily because Earth’s own orbit is recognized as one of those circles.

S_{2}=\{\,H_{3}^{*}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,H_{3}\}.

H_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Heliocentric Axiom 3: Uniform Circular Orbits (Copernican Ideal).}

Final Set (Heliocentric Axiom 3: Uniform Circular Orbits):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{H_{3}:\ \forall\,P:\ \mathrm{Orbit}(P)=\sum(\text{UniformCircles})\Bigr\}.}

Hence, the geocentric notion of a single star-sphere no longer prescribes daily orbits for all objects; the daily motion is Earth’s own spin, and planetary orbits are re-centered on the Sun with the classical assumption of uniform circular motion retained for each planet.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,G_{3}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{3}^{*}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}.

Interpretation of Each Step:

1.

Sphere of Fixed Stars
is replaced by the idea that star motion is apparent (due to Earth’s spin), so no actual rotating sphere is needed near Earth.

2.

Focus on planetary orbits around the Sun
, relegating stars to a distant background.

3.

Assert uniform circular orbits
for planets in a Copernican sense, retaining the classical ideal of perfect circles (or sums of them) for each planet’s path.

Thus, Geocentric Axiom 3 (a rotating sphere of stars around Earth) becomes Heliocentric Axiom 3 (each planet’s orbit around the Sun is still idealized as uniform circles), shifting from Earth-centered to Sun-centered geometry.

Q.3.4

Axiom 4

Goal:
Transform the
Geocentric Axiom 4

G_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,P\in\{\text{planets}\}:\ \mathrm{Orbit}(P)=\bigl(\text{deferent circle}\bigr)+\bigl(\text{epicycle circle}\bigr),

into the
Heliocentric Axiom 4

H_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S_{\star}:\ \Bigl(\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star},\text{Sun})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{planet},\text{Sun})\Bigr)\;\wedge\;(\text{stellar parallax }\approx 0),

which asserts that stars lie at immense distances, thereby explaining why stellar parallax is not observed with early instruments, while planetary retrograde motion is seen as a result of Earth’s orbit, not epicycles.

Initial Axiom (Geocentric, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{G_{4}\bigr\}}

G_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,P\in\{\text{planets}\}:\ \mathrm{Orbit}(P)=\bigl(\text{deferent}\bigr)+\bigl(\text{epicycle}\bigr).

In the Ptolemaic system, each planet’s apparent retrograde motion and brightness variations are explained by adding an epicycle (a smaller circle) whose center follows a larger circle (the deferent) around Earth.

Rewrite Step 1: Recognize Retrograde Motion is Simpler if Earth is Moving.

G_{4}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{4}^{\prime},

G_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{(Retrograde loops can be explained by Earth\textquoteright s orbital motion,}

so no elaborate epicycles are needed.)

Symbolically, once Earth is also orbiting (heliocentric insight), an outer planet’s apparent retrograde is just a relative motion effect when Earth “overtakes” that planet on the inside track.

S_{0}=\{\,G_{4}\}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{1}=\{\,G_{4}^{\prime}\}.

G_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Complex epicycles largely unnecessary if Earth\textquoteright s motion explains retrograde directly}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,G_{4}^{\prime}\}}

G_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Retrograde arises from relative orbital speeds; no dedicated epicycle needed}).

Rewrite Step 2: Address the “No Parallax Observed” Objection.

G_{4}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{4}^{*},

H_{4}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{(If Earth orbits the Sun, why no parallax?)\hskip 9.44456ptThe stars must be extremely distant.}

Symbolically, the main anti-heliocentric argument was “lack of stellar parallax.” The solution proposed: stars lie at vast distances such that parallax angles fall below detection thresholds of the era.

S_{1}=\{\,G_{4}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,H_{4}^{*}\}.

H_{4}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Stellar parallax is effectively zero if star distances are huge}).

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,H_{4}^{*}\}}

H_{4}^{*}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Immense star distances explain no visible parallax with available instruments}).

Rewrite Step 3: Formally Adopt Distant Stars and Negligible Parallax.

H_{4}^{*}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{4},

H_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S_{\star}:\ \Bigl(\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star},\text{Sun})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{planet},\text{Sun})\Bigr)\;\wedge\;(\text{stellar parallax}\approx 0).

Symbolically, we replace the geocentric notion of epicycles for planets with the recognition that any star parallax is too small to detect because stars reside at enormous distances compared to planetary orbits.

S_{2}=\{\,H_{4}^{*}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,H_{4}\}.

H_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Heliocentric Axiom 4: Stars are extremely distant, yielding negligible parallax.}

Final Set (Heliocentric Axiom 4: Distant Fixed Stars & Minimal Parallax):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{H_{4}:\,\exists\,S_{\star}:\,\bigl(\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star},\text{Sun})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{planet},\text{Sun})\bigr)\,\wedge\,(\text{parallax}\approx 0)\Bigr\}.}

Thus, the Ptolemaic epicycle requirement (for retrograde motion, brightness changes) is largely resolved by Earth’s motion in a Sun-centered system, while the apparent lack of parallax is explained by positing extremely large star distances.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,G_{4}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{4}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{4}^{*}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{4}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Epicycles
are largely unnecessary once Earth’s orbital motion explains retrograde loops.

2.

No observed stellar parallax
suggests stars are extremely distant.

3.

Hence
, we adopt
H_4
, stating stars lie at vast distances, producing negligible parallax.

Appendix R

From Heliocentric to Cosmological Model

R.1

Heliocentric Model

This section is the same as Section
Q.2
.

R.2

Cosmological Model

A modern cosmological model (as in the Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker class) describes the universe on large scales using the principles of homogeneity, isotropy, expansion, and the laws of relativistic gravity.
Below is a minimal and complete set of axioms, each of which is independent (i.e. it cannot be derived from the others) and collectively sufficient to derive the main structure of standard cosmology (Big Bang, cosmic expansion, redshift-distance relations, cosmic microwave background predictions).

R.2.1

Axioms

1.

Homogeneity

\forall\,(\text{comoving observers }O_{1},O_{2}),\hskip 9.24994pt\rho\bigl(O_{1},t\bigr)=\rho\bigl(O_{2},t\bigr),

where
\rho
is the average matter-energy density in a given large-scale region at cosmic time
t
.
No position in the universe is preferred on sufficiently large (cosmological) scales.

2.

Isotropy

\forall\,(\text{comoving observer }O),\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{directions }d_{1},d_{2}):\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Properties}(d_{1},O)=\mathrm{Properties}(d_{2},O).

No direction is special for a comoving observer on large scales; all directions appear statistically the same (e.g. same average temperature, same large-scale structure distribution).

3.

Expansion (Scale Factor)

\mathrm{Metric}(\tau)=a(\tau)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{2},

where
a(\tau)
is the scale factor (a function of cosmic time
\tau
) and
\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{2}
is a (fixed) 3D spatial metric of constant curvature.
All cosmologically relevant distances scale uniformly by
a(\tau)
over time.

4.

Relativistic Dynamics (Einstein Field Equations)

G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda\,g_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi\,G}{c^{4}}\;\,T_{\mu\nu},

where
G_{\mu\nu}
is the Einstein tensor,
\Lambda
is the cosmological constant,
g_{\mu\nu}
is the metric,
T_{\mu\nu}
is the stress-energy tensor of matter/energy, and
G
is Newton’s gravitational constant.
This governs how the scale factor
a(\tau)
evolves with cosmic time according to matter, radiation, and other energy contents.

R.2.2

Completeness

1.

Homogeneity & Isotropy.
(Axioms 1, 2) specify the large-scale distribution of matter/energy in the universe, ensuring no preferred locations or directions at cosmic scales.

2.

Expansion (Scale Factor).
(Axiom 3) asserts that the universe evolves via a time-dependent scale factor
a(\tau)
, scaling all cosmological distances, leading to redshifts of distant objects and the notion of cosmic epochs.

3.

Relativistic Dynamics.
(Axiom 4) connects the geometry of spacetime (
g_{\mu\nu}
,
\Lambda
) with the distribution of energy-momentum (
T_{\mu\nu}
), yielding the Friedmann equations in the homogeneous, isotropic case and governing how
a(\tau)
changes over time.

These four axioms provide the foundation for the standard cosmological model: from them we derive key results such as Hubble’s law, cosmic microwave background predictions, big-bang nucleosynthesis constraints, and the observed acceleration (if
\Lambda>0
).

R.2.3

Independence

1.

Homogeneity.
cannot be inferred from isotropy alone, nor from expansion or Einstein’s equations. One must explicitly state that all comoving positions are equivalent on large scales.

2.

Isotropy.
does not follow from homogeneity, expansion, or relativistic dynamics. A universe might be homogeneous but not isotropic (or vice versa). Isotropy around one point does not imply isotropy around all points without an axiom of homogeneity.

3.

Expansion (Scale Factor).
cannot be deduced from homogeneity, isotropy, or Einstein’s field equations alone. We must assume that cosmic distances scale with a single function
a(\tau)
—the simplest assumption consistent with observational data and the symmetry axioms.

4.

Relativistic Dynamics.
does not follow from any combination of homogeneity, isotropy, or scale-factor expansion. These geometric statements do not by themselves define how spacetime curvature and energy distribution evolve; the Einstein field equations are a separate physical postulate tying geometry to matter/energy.

R.3

Transformations

R.3.1

Axiom 1

Goal:
Transform a subset of Heliocentric Axioms

\bigl\{\,H_{4}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994ptH_{4}:\ \exists\,S_{\star}:\ \Bigl(\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star},\text{Sun})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\text{planet},\text{Sun})\Bigr)\;\wedge\;(\text{stellar parallax}\approx 0),

into the
Cosmological Axiom 1
(Homogeneity):

C_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{comoving observers }O_{1},O_{2}),\ \rho(O_{1},t)=\rho(O_{2},t),

which states that, on large scales, the universe has no preferred location and the matter/energy density is the same everywhere at any cosmic time
t
.
Symbolically, we show how acknowledging the vast distance to stars (and subsequently more distant nebulae, galaxies) implies no special center or boundary on large scales, leading to the principle that every place is equivalent in the cosmic view.

Initial Axiom (Heliocentric, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{\,H_{4}\bigr\}}

H_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S_{\star}:\ \Bigl(\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star},\text{Sun})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{planet},\text{Sun})\Bigr)\;\wedge\;(\text{stellar parallax}\approx 0).

This asserts that stars lie extremely far away compared to planetary orbits, producing negligible parallax.
Historically, it resolved the lack of observed parallax in a Sun-centered system.
But as telescopes improved, deeper observations (nebulae, galaxies) suggested an even vaster scale for the cosmos.

Rewrite Step 1: Extend “Far Away Stars” to “Countless Galaxies” at Even Greater Distances.

H_{4}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{4}^{\prime},

H_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{(Post-telescope era) }\mathrm{dist}(\text{galaxies})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\text{stars}),\hskip 9.24994pt

\text{revealing no obvious cosmic boundary}.

Symbolically, once astronomers found “spiral nebulae” were actually galaxies far beyond the Milky Way, it became clear that the universe extends vastly beyond any previously assumed star sphere.
No single location appears singled out as a center (the Sun is merely one star among billions in the Milky Way, itself among billions of galaxies).

S_{0}=\{\,H_{4}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}}

H_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{No obvious cosmic boundary}).

Rewrite Step 2: Recognize No Special Location in This Vast Distribution.

H_{4}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{1},

G_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{from any point of view, the large-scale view is statistically the same}).

Symbolically, the distribution of galaxies on very large scales (tens or hundreds of megaparsecs) appears roughly uniform, with no single cosmic center.
This leads to the notion that from any sufficiently large-scale region, the average matter distribution looks the same.

S_{1}=\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{1}\}.

G_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{No cosmic edge/center is apparent; large-scale structure is roughly uniform.}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{1}\}}

G_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Large-scale uniformity; each region of space sees a similar galaxy distribution}).

Rewrite Step 3: Formalize This “No Preferred Location” as Cosmic Homogeneity.

Finally, we state the modern
Homogeneity Axiom
:

G_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;C_{1},

where

C_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{comoving observers }O_{1},O_{2}),\ \rho(O_{1},t)=\rho(O_{2},t).

Symbolically, for any two observers moving with the cosmic flow (“comoving”), the matter/energy density they measure at a cosmic time
t
is the same.
No region of the universe is singled out on large scales, generalizing the old notion of “huge star distances” into a principle of uniform matter distribution.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{1}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,C_{1}\}.

C_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Cosmological Axiom 1: Homogeneity on large scales.}

Final Set (Cosmological Axiom 1: Homogeneity):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{C_{1}:\ \forall\,(\text{comoving }O_{1},O_{2}),\ \rho(O_{1},t)=\rho(O_{2},t)\Bigr\}.}

Hence, starting from the heliocentric concept that stars (and then galaxies) lie at immense distances with no near cosmic boundary (
H_{4}
), we generalize to an entire universe containing no privileged place or region, leading to the statement of
homogeneity
on the largest scales.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,H_{4}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{1}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,C_{1}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Very distant stars
in heliocentrism becomes
very distant galaxies
after telescopic discovery.

2.

No cosmic boundary/center
is apparent, leading to an essentially uniform distribution on vast scales.

3.

Hence
, we adopt
C_1
stating cosmic homogeneity: no location is favored at large scales.

R.3.2

Axiom 2

Goal:
Transform a subset of Heliocentric Axioms

\bigl\{\,H_{2}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994ptH_{2}:\ \mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\;\wedge\;\mathrm{Revolution}(\mathrm{Earth},T_{\oplus}),

into the
Cosmological Axiom 2
(
Isotropy
):

C_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{comoving observer }O),\ \forall\,(\text{directions }d_{1},d_{2}):\ \mathrm{Properties}(d_{1},O)=\mathrm{Properties}(d_{2},O).

This states that no direction in the universe is special on large scales; each observer sees essentially the same distribution of matter/energy in all directions.
Symbolically, we show how the notion of
Earth’s daily rotation and annual revolution
eventually generalizes to the idea that
no particular direction in the cosmos is privileged
, once we move beyond the solar system to cosmic scales.

Initial Axiom (Heliocentric, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{\,H_{2}\bigr\}}

H_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\;\wedge\;\mathrm{Revolution}(\mathrm{Earth},T_{\oplus}\approx 1\,\text{year}).

Here, Earth both spins on its axis daily and orbits the Sun annually.
Historically, this explains why the sky appears to rotate once per day (Earth’s spin) and why the Sun’s apparent path among the stars takes about one year (Earth’s orbital revolution).

Rewrite Step 1: Observational Consequence—“All Directions” on Earth’s Sky Are Visited.

H_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{2}^{\prime},

H_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{any given observer on Earth sees stars in all directions over time}).

Symbolically, because Earth rotates, an observer can eventually face every possible celestial direction (unless obstructed locally).
No particular direction in the sky remains fixed overhead, providing a hint that the cosmos is
not
oriented around one single direction from Earth’s viewpoint.

S_{0}=\{\,H_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,H_{2}^{\prime}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,H_{2}^{\prime}\}}

H_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{any given observer on Earth sees stars in all directions over time}).

Rewrite Step 2: Extend to Space Missions & Distant Observers—Still No Unique Direction.

H_{2}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{2},

where

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{No preferred cosmic axis found; all deep-space directions yield similar cosmic signals.}

Even away from Earth, probes/observers in solar orbits see no universal “axis” or “edge” in star distribution. From mid-20th century onward, spacecraft and telescopes confirm that in every direction of deep space, large-scale distributions of galaxies, cosmic background radiation, etc. appear statistically similar (once local structures are averaged out).
This suggests that
directional uniformity
is not an Earth artifact but an actual property of the universe on large scales.

S_{1}=\{\,H_{2}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}}

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{No preferred cosmic axis found; all deep-space directions yield similar cosmic signals.}

Rewrite Step 3: State This “No Direction is Special” as the Isotropy Axiom in Cosmology.

G_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;C_{2},

C_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{comoving observer }O),\ \forall\,(\text{directions }d_{1},d_{2}):\ \mathrm{Properties}(d_{1},O)=\mathrm{Properties}(d_{2},O).

Symbolically, any observer who moves with the cosmic flow sees that, on large scales, each direction is essentially the same: the same cosmic microwave background temperature, the same large-scale clustering of galaxies, etc.
No cosmic axis or cosmic pole is singled out, generalizing the “no privileged direction” idea from daily Earth rotation to the entire universe.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,C_{2}\}.

C_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Cosmological Axiom 2 (Isotropy): no direction is special on cosmic scales}.

Final Set (Cosmological Axiom 2: Isotropy):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{C_{2}:\ \forall\,(\text{comoving }O),\ \forall\,(d_{1},d_{2}),\ \mathrm{Properties}(d_{1},O)=\mathrm{Properties}(d_{2},O)\Bigr\}.}

Hence, starting from Earth’s rotation (which already implied no single direction in Earth’s sky is fundamentally preferred) and extending that logic plus deep-space observations, we reach the cosmological principle of
isotropy
: each direction is statistically the same at large scales.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,H_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{2}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,C_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Daily rotation
already hints no direction is
intrinsically
special from Earth’s perspective.

2.

Deep-space probes
confirm that, in all directions of the sky, cosmic structures and radiation are quite uniform.

3.

Therefore
, we adopt
C_2
:
Isotropy
at cosmic scales, meaning no direction is privileged for a comoving observer.

R.3.3

Axiom 3

Goal:
Transform a subset of Heliocentric Axioms

\bigl\{\,H_{4}\bigr\},\hskip 18.49988ptH_{4}:\;\exists\,S_{\star}:\;\Bigl(\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star},\text{Sun})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\text{planet},\text{Sun})\Bigr)\;\wedge\;(\text{stellar parallax}\approx 0),

into the
Cosmological Axiom 3
(
Expansion / Scale Factor
)

C_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Metric}(\tau)=a(\tau)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{2},

where
a(\tau)
is the scale factor (a function of cosmic time
\tau
) multiplying a constant–curvature spatial metric
\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{2}
.
Symbolically, we show how recognising ever-greater cosmic distances—and their systematic recession—leads to the idea that
all
large-scale distances grow proportionally with a single function
a(\tau)
.

Initial Axiom (Heliocentric, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{\,H_{4}\bigr\}}

H_{4}:\;\exists\,S_{\star}:\;\Bigl(\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star},\text{Sun})\gg\mathrm{dist}(\mathrm{planet},\text{Sun})\Bigr)\;\wedge\;(\text{stellar parallax}\approx 0).

This asserts that stars lie extremely far away compared with planetary orbits, producing negligible parallax.
Historically, it resolved the lack of observed parallax in a Sun-centred system, but as telescopes improved, deeper observations (nebulae, galaxies) suggested an even vaster cosmic scale.

Rewrite Step 1: Extend “Far-Away Stars” to “Countless Galaxies” at Even Greater Distances.

H_{4}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{4}^{\prime},

H_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{(Post-telescope era)}\;\mathrm{dist}(\text{galaxies})\gg\mathrm{dist}(S_{\star}),\hskip 18.49988pt\text{revealing no obvious cosmic boundary}.

Symbolically, once astronomers found “spiral nebulae” were actually galaxies far beyond the Milky Way, it became clear that the universe extends vastly beyond any previously assumed star sphere.
No single location appears singled out as a centre—the Sun is merely one star among billions in the Milky Way, itself among billions of galaxies.

S_{0}=\{\,H_{4}\}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{1}=\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}}

H_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{No obvious cosmic boundary}).

Rewrite Step 2: Empirical Redshift–Distance Relation (
Hubble’s Law
).

H_{4}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{3},

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{galaxy }G):\;v_{r}(G)=H_{0}\,d(G).

Here
d(G)
is the distance inferred (e.g. via Cepheids) and
H_{0}
is a universal constant (the present-day Hubble parameter).
This encodes the observation that
all
distant galaxies recede with a velocity proportional to their present distance.

S_{1}=\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}}

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{galaxy }G):\;v_{r}(G)=H_{0}\,d(G).

Rewrite Step 3: Interpret
v_{r}=H_{0}d
as Uniform Metric Expansion.

First write
d(\tau)=a(\tau)\chi
with comoving coordinate
\chi
; then

v_{r}=\dot{d}=\dot{a}\chi=\frac{\dot{a}}{a}d\hskip 9.24994pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptH(\tau)=\frac{\dot{a}}{a}.

Thus the empirical law implies that
all large-scale distances share the same multiplicative time factor
a(\tau)
:

G_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;C_{3},\hskip 18.49988ptC_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Metric}(\tau)=a(\tau)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{2}.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}\;\longrightarrow\;S_{3}=\{\,C_{3}\}.

Final Set (Cosmological Axiom 3 – Expansion / Scale Factor):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{C_{3}:\;\mathrm{Metric}(\tau)=a(\tau)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\Sigma^{2}\Bigr\}}

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form):

\underbrace{\{\,H_{4}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{4}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{3}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,C_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Very distant stars
in heliocentrism become
very distant galaxies
after telescopic discovery.

2.

Empirically
v_{r}=H_{0}d
, the
Hubble law
, holds for all galaxies.

3.

Therefore, distances evolve universally as
d(\tau)=a(\tau)\chi
, yielding the
Expansion / Scale-Factor
axiom
C_{3}
.

R.3.4

Axiom 4

Goal:
Transform a subset of Heliocentric Axioms

\bigl\{\,H_{1},\,H_{2}\bigr\}

where

H_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,P\neq\text{Sun}:\ \mathrm{CenterOrbit}(P)=\text{Sun},

H_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\;\wedge\;\mathrm{Revolution}(\mathrm{Earth},T_{\oplus}),

into the
Cosmological Axiom 4 (Relativistic Dynamics / Einstein Field Equations)
:

C_{4}:\hskip 9.24994ptG_{\mu\nu}\;+\;\Lambda\,g_{\mu\nu}\;=\;8\pi\,G\;T_{\mu\nu},

which states that spacetime curvature (via
G_{\mu\nu}
) is sourced by the stress-energy
T_{\mu\nu}
of matter/energy, with possible cosmological constant
\Lambda
. Symbolically, we show how the heliocentric ideas of
planetary orbits around the Sun
and
Earth’s dual motion
generalize into a fully
relativistic, global
law connecting geometry and matter on cosmic scales.

Initial Axioms (Heliocentric, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{\,H_{1},\,H_{2}\bigr\}}

H_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,P\neq\text{Sun}:\ \mathrm{CenterOrbit}(P)=\text{Sun},

H_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Rotation}(\mathrm{Earth},24\text{h})\;\wedge\;\mathrm{Revolution}(\mathrm{Earth},T_{\oplus}).

The first places the Sun at the center for all planets, while the second asserts Earth has a daily rotation and an annual revolution. Historically, these replaced the geocentric worldview with a simpler mechanical explanation for daily cycles and planetary wanderings, grounded in (pre-Newtonian) classical mechanics.

Rewrite Step 1: Newtonian Gravitation for the Solar System.

\{\,H_{1},H_{2}\}\;\longrightarrow\;H_{12}^{\prime},

H_{12}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Newton\textquoteright s laws + universal gravitation unify all planetary orbits around the Sun})\\

Symbolically, combining
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
in the 17
th
–18
th
century yields Newton’s framework: a gravitational law binding the solar system.
Thus, Earth’s rotation and revolution are no longer just kinematic facts but follow from universal gravitational dynamics.

S_{0}=\{\,H_{1},H_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,H_{12}^{\prime}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,H_{12}^{\prime}\}}

H_{12}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{Newton\textquoteright s laws + universal gravitation unify all planetary orbits around the Sun})\\

Rewrite Step 2: Need a Global Law for Gravity & Spacetime at Large Scales.

H_{12}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{4},

G_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Newton\textquoteright s laws are insufficient on cosmic/relativistic scales}

geometry of spacetime is dynamic and couples to energy distribution.

Symbolically, phenomena like Mercury’s perihelion shift, gravitational lensing, and especially cosmic expansion (Hubble’s law) push beyond Newton’s gravitational framework.
Hence,
a universal relativistic equation
relating spacetime geometry and mass-energy is needed.

S_{1}=\{\,H_{12}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{4}\}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{4}\}}

G_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Newton\textquoteright s laws are insufficient on cosmic/relativistic scales}

geometry of spacetime is dynamic and couples to energy distribution.

Rewrite Step 3: Postulate Einstein Field Equations Linking Geometry & Matter.

G_{4}\;\longrightarrow\;C_{4},

C_{4}:\hskip 9.24994ptG_{\mu\nu}\;+\;\Lambda\,g_{\mu\nu}\;=\;\frac{8\pi\,G}{c^{4}}\;\,T_{\mu\nu}.

Symbolically, the Einstein tensor
G_{\mu\nu}
(curvature) plus possible cosmological constant
\Lambda
equals the energy-momentum content
T_{\mu\nu}
scaled by
8\pi G/c^{4}
.
This universal law supersedes the local gravitational orbits concept, describing how
all
matter/energy sculpts spacetime on cosmic (and local) scales, leading to the Friedmann equations in a homogeneous and isotropic universe. The transformation of Newtonian laws into a general relativity framework is described in detail in Sec.
I
.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{4}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,C_{4}\}.

C_{4}:\hskip 9.24994ptG_{\mu\nu}\;+\;\Lambda\,g_{\mu\nu}\;=\;\frac{8\pi\,G}{c^{4}}\;\,T_{\mu\nu}.

Final Set (Cosmological Axiom 4: Einstein Field Equations):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{C_{4}:\ G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda\,g_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi\,G\,}{c^{4}}T_{\mu\nu}\Bigr\}.}

Thus, from heliocentric principles (
H_{1}

Sun-centered orbits
plus
H_{2}

Earth’s spin/revolution
) expanded by Newton’s universal gravitation, we move to a truly global statement:
spacetime geometry is shaped by mass-energy
, encapsulated by Einstein’s field equations, the heart of modern cosmology.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,H_{1},H_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,H_{12}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{4}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,C_{4}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Heliocentrism
implies local gravitational explanation (Newton), valid for solar system orbits.

2.

Relativistic phenomena
require a broader law of gravity and spacetime.

3.

Hence
, we adopt
C_4
:
Einstein’s field equations
govern the geometry and dynamics of the entire cosmos.

Appendix S

From Analog Photography to Digital Imaging

S.1

Analog Photography

Analog (film-based) photography relies on photosensitive chemical layers to record an image upon exposure to light, which is then revealed and fixed via chemical development.
Below is a minimal and complete set of axioms, each of which is independent (i.e. it cannot be derived from the others) and collectively sufficient to describe the classical film process, from capturing light onto film to producing a final negative or positive.

S.1.1

Axioms

1.

Photosensitive Medium

\exists\,F:\;F=(\text{emulsion of silver halides or similar light-reactive chemicals})

A physical film
F
(or plate/paper) contains an emulsion (for instance, a silver halide) that undergoes a chemical change upon exposure to photons.

2.

Exposure & Latent Image

\forall\,(\text{photon}\,\gamma,\ \text{site}\,s\in F):\hskip 9.24994pt(\gamma\ \text{impinges }s)\;\Longrightarrow\;\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}(s).

When light (photons) strikes the film at a given point
s
, it induces a latent chemical change proportional to the intensity and duration of exposure.

In the case of silver halides, exposure to light catalyses the decomposition of the compound into metallic silver.

3.

Chemical Development

\forall\,s\in F:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{latent }\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}(s))\;\xrightarrow[\text{fixer}]{\text{developer}}\;(\text{visible silver or dye formation}),

A chemical process (developer, stop bath, fixer) converts the latent changes into a stable, visible image (e.g. metallic silver grains, dye clouds).

More precisely, the developer amplifies the chemical change obtained after exposure, for instance, by reducing a silver halide to metallic silver in regions where a catalytic amount of silver is already present. The stop bath, because of low pH, makes the developer non-functional. Fixing (in the case of silver halides commonly done with thiosulfate) dissolves and washes away the unreacted emulsion, making the photograph light-resistant.

4.

Negative/Positive Image Formation

(\text{developed film }F)\hskip 9.24994pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{cases}(\text{negative})&\text{if silver-based on transparent base},\\
(\text{positive})&\text{if reversal or direct paper process}.\end{cases}

Depending on the process (negative film, reversal film, direct printing papers), the resulting image can be a negative (spots with high light intensity appear dark) or a positive (spots with low light intensity appear dark). Additional printing steps can yield positives from negatives and vice versa.

S.1.2

Completeness

1.

Photosensitive Medium.
(Axiom 1) establishes the physical film or paper coated with a light-reactive emulsion, the fundamental substrate of analog photography.

2.

Exposure & Latent Image.
(Axiom 2) ensures that light hitting the film creates an invisible (latent) record that reflects the intensity and duration of exposure.

3.

Chemical Development.
(Axiom 3) converts that latent pattern into a stable, visible image by chemical reduction or dye formation.

4.

Negative/Positive Formation.
(Axiom 4) describes how the final image is rendered—either as a negative on transparent base or a direct positive (via reversal or printing processes).

From these four, one can describe the entire workflow of traditional film photography: capturing an exposure, chemically developing it, and producing final negatives/prints.

S.1.3

Independence

1.

Photosensitive Medium.
cannot be inferred from the latent-image principle, development, or negative/positive outcome alone. One must explicitly state that a material exists which undergoes a chemical change upon exposure to light.

2.

Exposure & Latent Image.
is not deducible from having a photosensitive substrate, chemical development, or negative/positive processes by themselves. It specifically states how light intensity modifies the medium in an invisible, pre-development form.

3.

Chemical Development.
does not follow from the substrate or latent image existence, nor from final image polarity. It must be separately postulated that a chemical bath reveals and fixes the latent changes into a stable image.

4.

Negative/Positive Formation.
is not derivable from the first three axioms. Even with a photosensitive material, latent imaging, and development, one must specify whether the developed material represents a negative or direct positive, or whether an additional print step is used to invert/retain tonality.

S.2

Digital Imaging

Digital imaging captures, quantizes, and stores light information in discrete electronic form.
Unlike chemical reactions on film, digital sensors convert incoming photons into electronic signals sampled and quantized as pixel values.
Below is a minimal and complete set of axioms, each of which is independent (i.e. it cannot be derived from the others) and collectively sufficient to describe the fundamental process of digital image acquisition and storage.

S.2.1

Axioms

1.

Discrete Sensor Array

\exists\,S:\ S=(p_{ij})_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}},

where
p_{ij}
is a photosite (pixel element) arranged in an
M\times N
grid.
Each pixel
p_{ij}
independently collects incoming light for a finite integration time.

2.

Photoelectric Conversion

\forall\,p_{ij}\in S:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{photon }\gamma\text{ arrives at }p_{ij})\;\Longrightarrow\;(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}).

When a photon strikes photosite
p_{ij}
, it frees an electron or creates a charge proportional to light intensity, thus embedding a measurable signal in
p_{ij}
.

3.

Sampling & Quantization

\forall\,p_{ij}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}\bigr)\;\xrightarrow[\text{ADC}]{\text{Analog-Digital Converter}}\;\text{digital level }D_{ij}\in\{0,\dots,2^{b}-1\}.

An analog-to-digital converter (ADC) reads each pixel’s signal and maps it to a discrete integer
D_{ij}
(bit-depth
b
) representing brightness.

4.

Image File Creation

\{\,(D_{ij})\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}}\;\longrightarrow\;\mathrm{File}(\mathrm{format}),

Once all pixels are digitized, the set of discrete values
(D_{ij})
is stored or transmitted as an image file, potentially with compression, color encoding, or metadata.

S.2.2

Completeness

1.

Discrete Sensor Array.
(Axiom 1) ensures a finite matrix of independent photosensitive elements. This defines the basic hardware for digital capture.

2.

Photoelectric Conversion & Sampling.
Axiom 2 and Axiom 3 describe how photons create electrical signals in each pixel, then how these analog signals are discretized (quantized) into digital brightness values.

3.

Image File Creation.
(Axiom 4) completes the chain by storing or transmitting the array of digitized pixel values in a suitable image format, enabling display, editing, or distribution in the digital domain.

These four axioms cover the essential flow of digital imaging: capturing light on a sensor array, converting charges to discrete levels, and assembling these into a digital image file.

S.2.3

Independence

1.

Discrete Sensor Array.
cannot be deduced from the photoelectric or sampling axioms alone. One must explicitly state there is a 2D (or 1D) grid of discrete photosites rather than a continuous medium.

2.

Photoelectric Conversion.
does not follow from the existence of a sensor array or a file format. It specifically requires that incident photons generate measurable electronic charge, a separate physical principle.

3.

Sampling & Quantization.
is not implied by having a sensor array or photoelectric effect alone; it states that each analog pixel charge is read out and converted to an integer level.

4.

Image File Creation.
does not follow from the sensor, conversion, or quantization processes unless we explicitly posit the final step of assembling
(D_{ij})
into a digital file, with possible compression or metadata.

S.3

Transformations

S.3.1

Axiom 1

Goal:
Transform a subset of Analog Photography axioms

\bigl\{\,A_{1}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994ptA_{1}:\ \exists\,F:\;F=(\text{emulsion of silver halides or similar light-reactive chemicals}),

into the
Digital Imaging Axiom 1
:

D_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S:\ S=(p_{ij})_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}},

where
p_{ij}
is a photosite arranged in a discrete sensor array.
Symbolically, we show how the notion of a continuous, chemically reactive film medium yields to a grid of electronic photosites in digital capture.

Initial Axiom (Analog, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{1}\bigr\}}

A_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,F:\;F=(\text{emulsion of silver halides or similar light-reactive chemicals}),

stipulating that photographic film
F
has a photosensitive chemical layer that changes upon exposure to light.

Rewrite Step 1: Acknowledge Mechanism is Chemical
and
Continuous in Space.

A_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{1}^{\prime},

A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Film }F\text{ is coated with a continuous layer of reactive chemicals, not discrete pixels}.

Symbolically, the analog approach spreads the photosensitive reaction over a continuous emulsion.
No explicit “grid” structure is present.
The reaction intensity at any point on the film is a function of incident light.

S_{0}=\{\,A_{1}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime}\}.

A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Film }F\text{ is coated with a continuous layer of reactive chemicals, not discrete pixels}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime}\}}

A_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Film }F\text{ is coated with a continuous layer of reactive chemicals, not discrete pixels}.

Rewrite Step 2: Shift to Electronic Sensing Concept (Photoelectric Effect).

A_{1}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{1},

G_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{The capturing surface is subdivided into many small electronic \textquotedblleft sites\textquotedblright.}

Symbolically, we replace the continuous chemical reaction with discrete electronics: each site forms an independent “mini-sensor.”
This is still conceptual, not specifying an
M\times N
grid yet.

S_{1}=\{\,A_{1}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{1}\}.

G_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{The capturing surface is subdivided into many small electronic \textquotedblleft sites\textquotedblright.}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{1}\}}

G_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{The capturing surface is subdivided into many small electronic \textquotedblleft sites\textquotedblright.}

Rewrite Step 3: Assert a Discrete 2D Pixel Array in Digital Imaging.

G_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;D_{1},

D_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S:\ S=(p_{ij})_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}},

where each
p_{ij}
is a photosite in a finite grid (CCD, CMOS, etc.).
Symbolically, instead of a chemical layer, we now have a matrix of discrete electronic pixels.
All captured image information is sampled at these pixel sites.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{1}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,D_{1}\}.

D_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,S:\ S=(p_{ij})_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}},

Final Set (Digital Imaging Axiom 1: Discrete Sensor Array):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{D_{1}:\ \exists\,S:\ S=(p_{ij})_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}},\Bigr\}}

Hence, from analog’s continuous chemical film, we move to a discrete 2D sensor array—each pixel records local photon counts electronically.
No uniform chemical layer remains; each site is physically and electrically distinct.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,A_{1}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,A_{1}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{1}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,D_{1}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Analog film
is a continuous chemical medium.

2.

Move to concept
of multiple local photoelectric sites.

3.

Assert final discrete pixel grid
, the hallmark of digital sensor arrays.

Thus, Analog Axiom 1 (a photosensitive chemical film) transforms into Digital Axiom 1 (a finite matrix of electronic photosites) in the transition from chemical to electronic capture.

S.3.2

Axiom 2

Goal:
Transform a subset of Analog Photography axioms

\bigl\{\,A_{2}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994ptA_{2}:\ \forall\,(\text{photon}\,\gamma,\ \text{site}\,s\in F):\hskip 9.24994pt(\gamma\ \text{impinges }s)\;\Longrightarrow\;\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}(s),

into the
Digital Imaging Axiom 2
(Photoelectric Conversion):

D_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,p_{ij}\in S:\ (\text{photon }\gamma\ \text{arrives at }p_{ij})\;\Longrightarrow\;(e^{-}\text{ count of }p_{ij}\ \text{increases}).

Symbolically, we show how the analog concept of a chemical latent image (i.e. increased
\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}
) upon photon arrival is replaced by an electronic charge accumulation in each digital photosite.

Initial Axiom (Analog, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{2}\bigr\}}

A_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{photon}\,\gamma,\ \text{site}\,s\in F):\hskip 9.24994pt(\gamma\ \text{impinges }s)\;\Longrightarrow\;\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}(s).

Meaning: each photon hitting location
s
on the film triggers an incremental chemical change (latent image).
The strength of
\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}
accumulates with exposure.

Rewrite Step 1: Latent Chemical Change is Invisible Pre-Development.

A_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{2}^{\prime},

A_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Photon arrival modifies local chemistry, forming a latent (invisible) pattern.}

Symbolically, the response in analog film remains a hidden (latent) distribution of exposed silver halides or other reaction sites.
No immediate visible readout occurs until chemical steps are applied.

S_{0}=\{\,A_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}.

A_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Photon arrival modifies local chemistry, forming a latent (invisible) pattern.}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}}

A_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Photon arrival modifies local chemistry, forming a latent (invisible) pattern.}

Rewrite Step 2: Move from Chemical Reaction to Electronic Signal.

A_{2}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{2},

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{The response to photons is no longer a chemical shift, but an electrical effect.}

Symbolically, we replace the notion of
\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}
with
electron charge
increment.
No development step is needed to “make it visible”; the signal is already measurable electronically.

S_{1}=\{\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}.

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{The response to photons is no longer a chemical shift, but an electrical effect.}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}}

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{The response to photons is no longer a chemical shift, but an electrical effect.}

Rewrite Step 3: Finalize Photoelectric Conversion: Photon
\to
Electron Count/Current in Each Pixel.

G_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;D_{2},

D_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,p_{ij}\in S:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{photon }\gamma\ \text{hits }p_{ij})\;\Longrightarrow\;(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}).

Symbolically, each photon that arrives at the pixel
p_{ij}
contributes an electron or charge increment.
This is
digital
imaging’s second axiom:
photoelectric conversion
of light into electronic signals, supplanting the analog “latent chemical reaction.”

S_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,D_{2}\}.

D_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,p_{ij}\in S:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{photon }\gamma\ \text{hits }p_{ij})\;\Longrightarrow\;(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}).

Final Set (Digital Imaging Axiom 2: Photoelectric Conversion):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{D_{2}:\ \forall\,p_{ij}\in S:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{photon }\gamma\ \text{hits }p_{ij})\;\Longrightarrow\;(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}).\Bigr\}.}

Thus, from the chemical latent image principle (
A_{2}
) we progress to the concept of charge-based photon detection (
D_{2}
).
No invisible chemical shift remains; each pixel can be electronically read out to gauge how many photons were received.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,A_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,A_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{2}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,D_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Analog
film sees photon arrival as a
chemical latent change
.

2.

Electronic
approach replaces chemical shift with
charge
increments in each pixel.

3.

Hence
, we adopt
D_{2}
: photoelectric conversion accumulates an electri signal, enabling direct measurement.

S.3.3

Axiom 3

Goal:
Transform a subset of Analog Photography axioms

\bigl\{\,A_{3}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994ptA_{3}:\ \forall\,s\in F:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{latent }\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}(s))\;\xrightarrow[\text{developer}]{\text{chemical bath}}\;(\text{visible silver or dye formation}),

into the
Digital Imaging Axiom 3
(Sampling & Quantization):

D_{3}:\ \forall\,p_{ij}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}\bigr)\;\xrightarrow[\text{ADC}]{\text{Analog-Digital Converter}}\;D_{ij}\in\{0,\dots,2^{b}-1\}.

Symbolically, we show how chemical development (turning a latent image into visible silver/dyes) is replaced by an
analog-to-digital conversion
that produces a discrete brightness value for each pixel.

Initial Axiom (Analog, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{3}\bigr\}}

A_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,s\in F:\ (\text{latent }\Delta\!\mathrm{chem}(s))\;\xrightarrow[\text{developer}]{\text{chemical bath}}\;(\text{visible silver or dye formation}),

meaning once the film is developed in the proper chemicals, the latent chemical change at each site
s
becomes a stable, visible record of the exposure.

Rewrite Step 1: Chemical Development is an
Irreversible
Process Rendering a Visible Image.

A_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{3}^{\prime},

A_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Chemical Development is an \emph{Irreversible} Process Rendering a Visible Image}.

Symbolically, analog photography’s development (latent to visible silver/dye) is permanent and yields a continuous tonal representation. One cannot revert to the latent state, nor easily re-measure intensities—it’s a fixed analog record.

S_{0}=\{\,A_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,A_{3}^{\prime}\}.

A_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Chemical Development is an \emph{Irreversible} Process Rendering a Visible Image.}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,A_{3}^{\prime}\}}

A_{3}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Chemical Development is an \emph{Irreversible} Process Rendering a Visible Image.}.

Rewrite Step 2: Digital Sensor Produces an
Electronic
Signal, Not a Chemical Imprint.

A_{3}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{3},

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{No chemical \textquotedblleft development\textquotedblright needed; the readout from each pixel }p_{ij}\text{ is an electrical signal}.

Symbolically, in digital imaging, the “development” step is replaced by an
instant
electronic measurement. The pixel’s charge directly represents how much light was gathered, with no further chemical bath required.

S_{1}=\{\,A_{3}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}.

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{No chemical \textquotedblleft development\textquotedblright needed; the readout from each pixel }p_{ij}\text{ is an electrical signal}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}}

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{No chemical \textquotedblleft development\textquotedblright needed; the readout from each pixel }p_{ij}\text{ is an electrical signal}..

Rewrite Step 3: ADC Converts Analog Pixel Charge into Discrete Digital Levels (Sampling & Quantization).

G_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;D_{3},

D_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,p_{ij}:\ \bigl(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}\bigr)\;\xrightarrow[\text{ADC}]{\text{Analog-Digital Converter}}\;D_{ij}\in\{0,\dots,2^{b}-1\}.

Symbolically, this is digital imaging’s “development”: each pixel’s analog voltage/charge is sampled and quantized into an integer level
D_{ij}
(with bit-depth
b
). Hence, we no longer rely on silver grains or dyes to “store” brightness but a numeric code in memory.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,D_{3}\}.

D_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,p_{ij}:\ \bigl(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}\bigr)\;\xrightarrow[\text{ADC}]{\text{Analog-Digital Converter}}\;D_{ij}\in\{0,\dots,2^{b}-1\}.

Final Set (Digital Imaging Axiom 3: Sampling & Quantization):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{D_{3}:\ \forall\,p_{ij}:\ \bigl(\text{electric signal at }p_{ij}\bigr)\;\xrightarrow{\text{ADC}}\;D_{ij}\in\{0,\dots,2^{b}-1\}.\Bigr\}.}

Hence, the analog development process (
A_{3}
) is replaced by an electronic readout +
ADC step
(
D_{3}
) that yields integer-coded pixel intensities. No permanent chemical transformation is required; the brightness values become discrete digital data.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,A_{3}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,A_{3}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{3}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,D_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Analog
step: latent chemical image
\to
chemical development
\to
visible silver/dye.

2.

Digital
step: sensor accumulates charge, read out as an analog voltage.

3.

Quantization
: an ADC produces discrete integer values for each pixel, supplanting chemical development.

S.3.4

Axiom 4

Goal:
Transform a subset of Analog Photography axioms

\bigl\{\,A_{4}\bigr\}\hskip 9.24994ptA_{4}:\ (\text{developed film }F)\ \Longrightarrow\ \begin{cases}(\text{negative}),&\text{if silver-based on transparent base},\\
(\text{positive}),&\text{if reversal or direct paper process}.\end{cases}

into the
Digital Imaging Axiom 4
(Image File Creation):

D_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl\{\,(D_{ij})\bigr\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}}\ \longrightarrow\ \mathrm{File}(\mathrm{format}),

which states that once all pixels have been digitized (
D_{ij}
values), they are stored or transmitted as an image file (e.g. JPEG, RAW, PNG). Symbolically, we show how the analog concept of ending with a physical negative/positive is replaced by generating a purely
digital file
containing pixel data.

Initial Axiom (Analog, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{A_{4}\bigr\}}

A_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{developed film }F)\hskip 9.24994pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{cases}(\text{negative}),&\text{silver-based on transparent substrate},\\
(\text{positive}),&\text{reversal or direct print process}.\end{cases}

Meaning: once film is chemically developed, the result is a physical negative or positive. Additional steps (e.g. printing) can yield a positive from a negative.

Rewrite Step 1: Analog Final Stage = Physical Medium with Fixed Tonality.

A_{4}\;\longrightarrow\;A_{4}^{\prime},

A_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{End product is a tangible film or paper with permanent density or color distribution}.

Symbolically, the analog approach yields a
physical
artifact (negative or positive). Its tonality is baked in by chemical processes and physically resides on film/paper.

S_{0}=\{\,A_{4}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,A_{4}^{\prime}\}.

A_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{End product is a tangible film or paper with permanent density or color distribution}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,A_{4}^{\prime}\}}

A_{4}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{End product is a tangible film or paper with permanent density or color distribution}.

Rewrite Step 2: In Digital Workflow, No Negative/Positive Medium—Instead, We Have Pixel Data.

A_{4}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{4},

G_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{End product has pure numeric pixel data, no physical negative or positive needed.}

Symbolically, the “final stage” of capturing an image in digital form is
a matrix of brightness (and possibly color) integers
, not a negative or positive film. The concept of “tonal inversion” can be done mathematically, not physically.

S_{1}=\{\,A_{4}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{4}\}.

G_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{End product has pure numeric pixel data, no physical negative or positive needed.}

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{4}\}}

G_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{End product has pure numeric pixel data, no physical negative or positive needed.}

Rewrite Step 3: Store or Transmit Pixel Data as an Image File (Digital Axiom 4).

G_{4}\;\longrightarrow\;D_{4},

D_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl\{\,(D_{ij})\bigr\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}}\ \longrightarrow\ \mathrm{File}(\mathrm{format}).

Symbolically, once the pixel values
(D_{ij})
are obtained, they are either saved in a file (like JPEG, RAW, PNG) or transmitted over a network, possibly with compression and metadata. No physical negative/positive is required; all manipulations (inversion, color grading) are done in software.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{4}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,D_{4}\}.

D_{4}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl\{\,(D_{ij})\bigr\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,M\\
j=1,\dots,N\end{subarray}}\ \longrightarrow\ \mathrm{File}(\mathrm{format}).

Final Set (Digital Imaging Axiom 4: Image File Creation):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{D_{4}:\ (D_{ij})\longrightarrow\mathrm{File}(\mathrm{format})\Bigr\}.}

Thus, from the analog concept of ending up with a physical negative or positive (
A_{4}
), we shift to storing discrete pixel values in a digital file (
D_{4}
). Any “negative” or “positive” effect is purely a matter of processing the numeric data.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,A_{4}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,A_{4}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{4}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,D_{4}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Analog
final step: a physical negative/positive on film or paper.

2.

Digital
final step: an array of pixel values in memory, no inherent negative/positive.

3.

Hence
, we adopt
D_{4}
: an image file with discrete pixel data replaces the physical end-product.

Appendix T

From Conventional Metals to Metamaterials

T.1

Conventional Metals

Conventional metals are continuous, uniform conductors whose properties arise from a free-electron gas within a crystalline lattice. Below is a minimal (but not strictly limited to four) set of axioms, each of which is independent (i.e. it cannot be derived from the others) and collectively sufficient to capture classical metallic behavior: strong conduction, predictable frequency response, and robust reflection/skin effect.

T.1.1

Axioms

1.

Uniform Bulk Medium

\forall\,(\text{metal volume }V):\hskip 9.24994pt\text{no engineered micro- or nano-scale patterns at }\,d\ll\lambda.

The metallic material is treated as homogeneous at the wavelength(s) of interest. Any microscopic lattice structure is on atomic scales, not artificially arranged to yield unusual wave properties.

2.

Free Electron Gas

\exists\,n_{e}>0:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{mobile conduction electrons with density }n_{e},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{weakly bound to ion cores}.

These electrons are responsible for electric current under applied fields, moving through a largely fixed ionic lattice.

3.

Ohmic Conduction (Low-Frequency Limit)

\forall\,(\mathbf{E}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}):\hskip 9.24994pt\mathbf{J}=\sigma\,\mathbf{E},

where
\mathbf{E}
is the applied electric field,
\mathbf{J}
is the current density, and
\sigma
is a (real, positive) conductivity constant for DC or low-frequency fields.

4.

Drude Model for AC Fields

\sigma(\omega)=\frac{n_{e}e^{2}}{m}\,\frac{1}{\Gamma-i\,\omega},

where
m
is the electron mass,
\Gamma
is the electron collision rate, and
\omega
is the angular frequency of the field.
This simplest classical model captures the inertia and damping of electrons, yielding frequency-dependent conductivity.

5.

High Reflectivity & Skin Effect

\mathrm{Reflection}(\omega)\approx 1\,(\text{over wide bands}),\,\,\mathrm{SkinDepth}(\delta)=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\mu_{0}\,\sigma\,\omega}\Bigr(\sqrt{1+{\Bigl(\frac{\omega\epsilon}{\sigma}\Bigr)}^{2}}+\frac{\omega\epsilon}{\sigma}}\Bigr).

Metals reflect most incident electromagnetic waves in the relevant frequency range, and the fields only penetrate a small distance (skin depth) inside.
\omega,\sigma,\epsilon,\mu_{0}
are the angular frequency of the current, the conductivity of the metal, its permittivity, and vacuum permeability, respectively.

6.

Standard Wave Phenomena

\mathrm{Re}[\epsilon(\omega)]<0,\ \mathrm{Re}[\mu(\omega)]\approx\mu_{0}\,\,(\text{over wide bands}),\,\,\text{leading to normal metallic responses.}

Permittivity is typically negative over a range of frequencies (ensuring strong reflection), while permeability remains close to that of free space.
No anomalous wave behaviors (e.g. negative index or hyperbolic dispersion) arise in a metal.

T.1.2

Completeness

1.

Uniform Bulk Medium.
(Axiom 1) stipulates no deliberate microstructures that might produce unusual wave effects. The metal is homogeneous on scales above the atomic lattice.

2.

Free Electron Gas.
(Axiom 2) provides the reservoir of mobile electrons responsible for conduction and collective plasma-like responses.

3.

Ohmic Conduction & Drude Model.
(Axioms 3 & 4) specify how metals conduct at DC/low-frequency (Ohm’s law) and how the conduction generalizes to AC fields (Drude model), covering inertia and collision processes of electrons.

4.

High Reflectivity, Skin Effect, & Standard Wave Phenomena.
(Axioms 5 & 6) ensure metals reflect electromagnetic waves strongly, have a small penetration depth, and do not exhibit exotic wave propagation or negative refraction under normal conditions.

T.1.3

Independence

1.

Uniform Bulk Medium.
cannot be deduced from the presence of free electrons or from conduction/reflectivity principles alone. One must explicitly posit that the metal is not deliberately structured above atomic scales.

2.

Free Electron Gas.
is not guaranteed by homogeneity, ohmic conduction, or reflectivity. It specifically asserts a high density of electrons that move freely and produce conduction.

3.

Ohmic Conduction & Drude Model.
do not follow from homogeneity or electron presence alone. One must separately state that conduction is linear at low frequency and extends to AC fields via inertia/collisions.

4.

High Reflectivity, Skin Effect, & Standard Wave Phenomena.
are not implied by the other axioms unless explicitly stated. Even with a free electron model, one must specify that metals typically exhibit near-unity reflection and do not support unusual wave effects in the absence of subwavelength structuring.

T.2

Metamaterials

Metamaterials are artificially engineered structures with subwavelength inclusions designed to yield effective electromagnetic (or other wave-based) properties not found in conventional materials.
They rely on structured unit cells whose size is smaller than the operating wavelength, allowing them to be treated as a uniform medium with unusual permittivity, permeability, or other effective parameters.

T.2.1

Axioms

1.

Subwavelength Structuring

\exists\,\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{N}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl(\text{each unit cell }\mathcal{U}_{k}\text{ has size }d\ll\lambda\bigr),

where
d
is the characteristic dimension of each unit cell and
\lambda
is the operational wavelength.
All metamaterial elements are arranged in a periodic or quasi-periodic lattice, ensuring
d\ll\lambda
so that wave interactions average out at macroscopic scales.

2.

Effective Medium Approximation

\forall\,(\omega,\ \mathbf{k}):\hskip 9.24994pt\text{effective parameters}\ \{\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\,\mu_{\text{eff}},\dots\},

meaning each lattice cell’s response can be homogenized into bulk permittivity/permeability (or analogous properties) over scales larger than
d
, yielding an effective medium approach.

3.

Designable Parameters

\forall\,(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}}):\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,(\text{unit cell geometry }G):\hskip 9.24994pt(\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\,\mu_{\text{eff}})\approx(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}}).

Through choice of subwavelength inclusions, geometry
G
, and material composition, one can tailor the resulting effective permittivity/permeability to achieve targeted wave properties (e.g. negative index, chirality, etc.).

4.

Exotic Wave Phenomena

\exists\,\text{(metamaterial)}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathbf{k},\,\omega)\ \text{relations}\notin\{\text{conventional materials}\}.

Because of the artificially engineered effective parameters, metamaterials can support wave propagation phenomena not observed in standard media (e.g. negative refraction, hyperbolic dispersion, cloaking effects).

T.2.2

Completeness

1.

Subwavelength Structuring.
(Axiom 1) posits that each unit cell is significantly smaller than the working wavelength, allowing a continuous “effective medium” description at larger scales.

2.

Effective Medium Approximation.
(Axiom 2) ensures the metamaterial can be treated as possessing bulk electromagnetic parameters (
\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}
, etc.) once homogenized across many unit cells.

3.

Designable Parameters.
(Axiom 3) underlies the metamaterial concept: by engineering each subwavelength structure, one can realize unconventional values of
\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}
(even negative or anisotropic) not found in natural materials.

4.

Exotic Wave Phenomena.
(Axiom 4) follows from these engineered effective properties, enabling negative refraction, superlensing, cloaking, or other wave effects outside the usual range of “conventional” media.

These four axioms capture the essence of metamaterials: artificial subwavelength structuring yields an emergent, homogenized medium with highly tunable electromagnetic behavior, enabling wave phenomena unattainable in conventional materials.

T.2.3

Independence

1.

Subwavelength Structuring.
is not implied by homogenization, designable parameters, or exotic wave phenomena alone. One must explicitly state that the lattice cells are much smaller than
\lambda
, enabling an effective medium model.

2.

Effective Medium Approximation.
cannot be derived simply from subwavelength structuring, design goals, or observed exotic phenomena. It specifically asserts that these discrete inclusions behave
as if
a continuous medium at the macroscopic scale.

3.

Designable Parameters.
does not follow automatically from subwavelength structuring, homogenization, or exotic effects. One must posit that, by altering inclusions’ geometry and composition,
\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}
can be tuned to near-target values.

4.

Exotic Wave Phenomena.
is not guaranteed by subwavelength structures, homogenization, or tunability alone. While these enable novel parameter regimes, actually exhibiting negative index or hyperbolic dispersion requires a separate axiom stating that such wave solutions exist under the effective parameters.

T.3

Transformations

T.3.1

Axiom 1

Goal:
Transform the
Uniform Bulk Medium
axiom from conventional metals:

M_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{metal volume }V):\ \text{no engineered micro- or nano-scale patterns at }d\ll\lambda,

into the
Subwavelength Structuring
axiom in metamaterials:

MM_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\}:\ \bigl(\text{each unit cell }\mathcal{U}_{k}\text{ has }d\ll\lambda\bigr),

meaning metamaterials deliberately include subwavelength inclusions or cells that replace the notion of a uniform bulk metal.

Initial Axiom (Conventional Metal, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{M_{1}\bigr\}}

M_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\text{metal volume }V),\hskip 9.24994pt\text{no micro- or nano-scale patterns at }d\ll\lambda.

This states that a conventional metal is taken as uniform at wavelengths of interest: it has no deliberately structured inclusions to alter wave behavior beyond normal conduction and reflection.

Rewrite Step 1: Recognize the Potential for Micro-/Nanostructuring to Influence Wave Properties.

M_{1}\;\longrightarrow\;M_{1}^{\prime},

M_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Structured inclusions at }d\ll\lambda\text{ possible}.

Symbolically, from a uniform metal viewpoint, no design freedom exists at subwavelength scales. However, if we relax that condition and consider adding small-scale structures, we open a path to controlling effective electromagnetic response.

S_{0}=\{\,M_{1}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,M_{1}^{\prime}\}.

M_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Structured inclusions at }d\ll\lambda\text{ possible}.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,M_{1}^{\prime}\}}

M_{1}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Structured inclusions at }d\ll\lambda\text{ possible}.

Rewrite Step 2: Introduce Concept of Unit Cells (Artificial Inclusions) Arranged Periodically.

M_{1}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;MM_{1},

MM_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{N}:\ \text{each unit cell }\mathcal{U}_{k}\text{ has dimension }d\ll\lambda,

Symbolically, we move from an implicit uniform medium to an explicitly
engineered
structure, by means of its unit cells
\mathcal{U}_{k}
. Each cell
\mathcal{U}_{k}
is small compared to the wavelength
\lambda
but large enough to be patterned with conductive or dielectric elements.

S_{1}=\{\,M_{1}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,MM_{1}\}.

MM_{1}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{N}:\ \text{each unit cell }\mathcal{U}_{k}\text{ has dimension }d\ll\lambda,

Final Set (Metamaterial Axiom 1: Subwavelength Structuring):

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{MM_{1}:\ \exists\,\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\},\ d\ll\lambda\Bigr\}.}

Hence, from the conventional metal’s axiom of “no subwavelength patterns” we transition to a metamaterial principle of
subwavelength unit cells
intentionally designed. This step is key to enabling
effective medium
approaches with novel wave behaviors.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,M_{1}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,M_{1}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,MM1_{1}\}}_{S_{2}}

1.

Conventional metal
is uniform at relevant wavelengths (
M_{1}
).

2.

Possible segmentation
suggests adding structured inclusions if
d\ll\lambda
.

3.

Hence
,
MM_{1}
states that metamaterials
do
incorporate subwavelength cells, enabling engineered properties.

T.3.2

Axiom 2

Goal:
Transform the
Free Electron Gas
axiom from conventional metals:

M_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,n_{e}>0:\text{mobile conduction electrons with density }n_{e},

into the
Effective Medium Approximation
axiom in metamaterials:

MM_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\omega,\mathbf{k}):\text{the structured composite can be described by}\ \{\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}},\dots\}.

Symbolically, we replace the idea that metal conductivity is solely from a dense electron gas in a homogeneous lattice with the notion that
structured
inclusions yield a
homogenized
set of bulk parameters at the macroscopic scale.

Initial Axiom (Conventional Metal, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{M_{2}\bigr\}}

M_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\,n_{e}>0:\ \text{mobile conduction electrons with density }n_{e},

This asserts that conduction in a conventional metal stems from electrons in a uniform host, without additional structural variations at subwavelength scales.
