Rewrite Step 1: Note That Conduction Need Not Only Arise from Electron Gas; We Could Use Structured Elements.

M_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;M_{2}^{\prime},

M_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl(\text{electrons exist, but local resonators, inclusions, or dielectrics could shape fields}\bigr).

Symbolically, a straightforward free-electron gas assumption no longer suffices if we embed other materials or patterns that can store and release electromagnetic energy differently (e.g., split-ring resonators, rods, capacitive/inductive elements).

S_{0}=\{\,M_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,M_{2}^{\prime}\}.

M_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl(\text{electrons exist, but local resonators, inclusions, or dielectrics could shape fields}\bigr).

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,M_{2}^{\prime}\}}

M_{2}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\bigl(\text{electrons exist, but local resonators, inclusions, or dielectrics could shape fields}\bigr).

Rewrite Step 2: Collective Response of These Cells Can Behave Like a Uniform “Effective Medium.”

M_{2}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{2},

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Local conduction/dielectric responses unify into macroscopic }

\text{permittivity/permeability, if }d\ll\lambda.

Symbolically, even though each unit cell might have metal regions, dielectric gaps, resonators, etc., at a scale
d\ll\lambda
, we can approximate the entire composite as a continuous medium with effective bulk properties.

S_{1}=\{\,M_{2}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}.

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Local conduction/dielectric responses unify into macroscopic }

\text{permittivity/permeability, if }d\ll\lambda.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}}

G_{2}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Local conduction/dielectric responses unify into macroscopic }

\text{permittivity/permeability, if }d\ll\lambda.

Rewrite Step 3: Finalize Metamaterial Axiom—Effective Medium Approximation (
MM_{2}
).

G_{2}\;\longrightarrow\;MM_{2},

MM_{2}:\,\forall\,(\omega,\mathbf{k}),\ \text{metamaterial has effective parameters}\ \{\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}\}

over scales larger than the cell size
d\ll\lambda
.
Thus, the notion of a simple electron gas is replaced by a broader “homogenized” set of parameters capturing conduction
and
other local resonances or inclusions.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{2}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,MM_{2}\}.

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{MM_{2}:\,\forall\,(\omega,\mathbf{k}),\ \text{metamaterial has effective parameters}\ \{\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}\}\Bigr\}.}

Hence, from the purely “free-electron conduction in a uniform lattice” viewpoint (
M_{2}
), we evolve to the metamaterial “effective medium” principle (
MM_{2}
), allowing diverse microstructures that collectively behave as a uniform material with tunable properties.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,M_{2}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,M_{2}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{2}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,MM_{2}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Conventional metal
has a simple free-electron conduction model (
M_{2}
).

2.

Local structuring
introduces varied responses besides plain electron gas.

3.

Hence
,
MM_{2}
states wavefields can be homogenized into an
effective
bulk medium with
\{\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}\}
.

T.3.3

Axiom 3

Goal:
Transform the
Standard Wave Phenomena
axiom of conventional metals:

M_{6}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\omega):\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Re}[\epsilon(\omega)]<0,\ \mathrm{Re}[\mu(\omega)]\approx\mu_{0},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{leading to normal (non-exotic) responses},

into the
Designable Parameters
axiom for metamaterials:

MM_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}}):\;\exists\,(\text{unit cell geometry }G):\hskip 9.24994pt(\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\,\mu_{\text{eff}})\approx(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}}).

Symbolically, we replace the fixed, intrinsic wave behavior of a uniform metal with the idea that subwavelength structuring can
tune
the effective permittivity/permeability to virtually any desired values.

Initial Axiom (Conventional Metal, Subset):

\boxed{S_{0}=\bigl\{M_{6}\bigr\}}

M_{6}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\omega):\hskip 9.24994pt\mathrm{Re}[\epsilon(\omega)]<0,\ \mathrm{Re}[\mu(\omega)]\approx\mu_{0},\text{leading to normal (non-exotic) responses},\hskip 9.24994pt

This asserts that in a
uniform
metal, the material parameters are not deliberately manipulated to produce unusual or “designer” wave properties.
Negative real permittivity is natural to metals in some bands, but
\mathrm{Re}[\mu(\omega)]\approx\mu_{0}
is nearly constant, precluding negative refraction, hyperbolic dispersion, etc.

Rewrite Step 1: Recognize That Material Parameters Need Not Be Fixed.

M_{6}\;\longrightarrow\;M_{6}^{\prime},

M_{6}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Though metals have \textquotedblleft standard\textquotedblright wave phenomena, one might alter structure}\\

to achieve non-standard (e.g.,
\epsilon<0
&
\mu<0
) or anisotropic dispersion.

Symbolically, the locked-down wave properties of an unstructured metal can be overcome if we abandon the presumption that
\mathrm{Re}[\mu(\omega)]\approx\mu_{0}
is final.
We can embed shapes or resonators that shift
effective
permittivity/permeability from their normal metallic values.

S_{0}=\{\,M_{6}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{1}=\{\,M_{6}^{\prime}\}.

M_{6}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Though metals have \textquotedblleft standard\textquotedblright wave phenomena, one might alter structure}\\

to achieve non-standard (e.g.,
\epsilon<0
&
\mu<0
) or anisotropic dispersion.

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{1}=\{\,M_{6}^{\prime}\}}

M_{6}^{\prime}:\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Though metals have \textquotedblleft standard\textquotedblright wave phenomena, one might alter structure}\\

to achieve non-standard (e.g.,
\epsilon<0
&
\mu<0
) or anisotropic dispersion.

Rewrite Step 2: Introduce the Idea that Subwavelength Cell Geometry Enables Tailoring of Effective Parameters.

M_{6}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{3},

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{by selecting each unit cell\textquoteright s shapes/materials, we set }\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}\text{ to desired goals}).

Symbolically, rather than being forced to accept the natural metallic
\epsilon(\omega)
and
\mu(\omega)
, we can place carefully chosen resonators (e.g. split rings, wires) inside each small cell to tune the effective wave response at scale
d\ll\lambda
.

S_{1}=\{\,M_{6}^{\prime}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}.

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{by selecting each unit cell\textquoteright s shapes/materials }\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}\text{ can be set to desired values}).

Current Set:

\boxed{S_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}}

G_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{by selecting each unit cell\textquoteright s shapes/materials }\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\mu_{\text{eff}}\text{ can be set to desired values}).

Rewrite Step 3: Finalize Metamaterial Axiom—Designable Parameters (
MM_{3}
).

G_{3}\;\longrightarrow\;MM_{3},

MM_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}})\,\exists\,(metamaterial):\hskip 9.24994pt(\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\,\mu_{\text{eff}})\approx(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}}).

Hence, if we can shape the subwavelength inclusions appropriately, we can approximate any real/fictitious combination of permittivity/permeability, enabling wave phenomena well beyond normal metallic reflection or conduction.

S_{2}=\{\,G_{3}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\longrightarrow\hskip 9.24994ptS_{3}=\{\,MM_{3}\}.

\boxed{S_{3}=\Bigl\{MM_{3}:\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\,(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}})\,\exists\,(metamaterial):\hskip 9.24994pt(\epsilon_{\text{eff}},\,\mu_{\text{eff}})\approx(\epsilon_{\text{target}},\,\mu_{\text{target}}).\Bigr\}.}

Thus, from the “standard wave phenomena” axiom in metals (where
\epsilon,\mu
are largely fixed) we transition to metamaterials’
designable parameters
principle, stating that effective permittivity/permeability can be tailored to match almost any target combination.

Symbolic Evolution (Compact Form)
:

\underbrace{\{\,M_{6}\}}_{S_{0}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,M_{6}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,G_{3}\}}_{S_{2}}~\longrightarrow~\underbrace{\{\,MM_{3}\}}_{S_{3}}.

1.

Metal
has standard wave phenomena (
M_{6}
) with limited
\epsilon(\omega)
,
\mu(\omega)
variety.

2.

Structured cells
can surpass these intrinsic limitations (
M_{6}^{\prime}
,
G_{3}
).

3.

Hence
,
MM_{3}
asserts full
designability
of effective parameters, achieving novel wave behaviors.

T.3.4

Axiom 4

Goal:
Transform the metallic axiom

\displaystyle M_{6}

\displaystyle:\,\mathrm{Re}[\epsilon(\omega)]<0,\ \mathrm{Re}[\mu(\omega)]\approx\mu_{0}\,\,(\text{over wide bands}),

into the metamaterial axiom of
Exotic Wave Phenomena

MM_{4}:\;\exists\,\text{(metamaterial)}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathbf{k},\,\omega)\ \text{relations}\notin\{\text{conventional materials}\}.

Initial set:

\boxed{S_{0}=\{M_{6}:\mathrm{Re}[\epsilon(\omega)]<0,\ \mathrm{Re}[\mu(\omega)]\approx\mu_{0}\,\,(\text{over wide bands}),\}}

Rewrite Step 1: Enable full parameter control.

Using a close analog of
M_{6}^{\prime}
(see derivation of Axiom 3) removes restrictions on dispersion.

M_{6}\;\longrightarrow\;M_{6}^{\prime}.

M_{6}^{\prime}:(\epsilon,\mu)\text{ are tunable via engineered structuring (unlike in metals)}

Current set

\boxed{S_{1}=\{M_{6}^{\prime}:(\epsilon,\mu)\text{ are tunable via engineered structuring (unlike in metals)}\}}

Rewrite Step 2: Realise non
-
standard dispersion.

Tuned parameters (
M_{6}^{\prime}
) can generate hyperbolic or negative
-
index bands.

M_{6}^{\prime}\;\longrightarrow\;G_{4}

G_{4}:\;\exists M:\;(\mathbf{k},\omega)\text{-relations are hyperbolic or give negative refraction (unlike in metals)}.

Current set

\boxed{S_{2}=\{G_{4}:\}}

G_{4}:\;\exists M:\;(\mathbf{k},\omega)\text{-relations are hyperbolic or give negative refraction (unlike in metals)}.

Rewrite Step 3: Declare the metamaterial axiom 4.

Since engineered behavior, such as a negative coefficient of refraction, is not observed in conventional metals, one can explicitly state the metamaterial axiom 4.

G_{4}\;\longrightarrow\;MM_{4}.

Final set

\boxed{S_{3}=\{MM_{4}:\exists\,\text{(metamaterial)}:\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathbf{k},\,\omega)\ \text{relations}\notin\{\text{conventional materials}\}.\}}

Symbolic evolution (compact view):

\underbrace{\{M_{6}\}}_{S_{0}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{M_{6}^{\prime}\}}_{S_{1}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{G_{4}\}}_{S_{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\{MM_{4}\}}_{S_{3}}.

In summary, the conventional constraints on material properties have gradually been loosened, ultimately leading to the metamaterial axiom that permits structures exhibiting non-standard phenomena.
